Questão 1

§   



 




I.



The assumption that a hull form that is optimum in calm water will also be optimum in rough seas is not necessarily valid.






II.



The effect of sea condition is customarily allowed for by the provision of a service power margin above the power required in rough water, an allowance which depends on the type of ship and the average weather on the sea routes on which the ship is designed to operate.






III.



The determination of this service allowance depends on the accumulation of sea-performance data on similar ships in similar trades.

D) Apenas as afirmativas (I) e (II) são corretas

Questão 8

The total resistance is made up of a number of different components, which are caused by a variety of factors and which interact one with the other in an extremely complicated way. In order to deal with the question more simply, it is usual to consider the total calm water resistance as being made up of four main components: (Chapter V - Section 1, p.2)


§   



 




I.



The frictional resistance, due to the motion of the hull through a viscous fluid.






II.



The wave-making resistance, due to the energy that must be supplied continuously by the ship to the wave system created on the surface of the water.






III.



Eddy resistance, due to the energy carried away by eddies shed from the hull or appendages. Local eddying will occur behind appendages such as bossings, shafts and shaft struts, and from stern frames and rudders if these items are not properly streamlined and aligned with the flow. Also, if the after end of the ship is too blunt, the water may be unable to follow the curvature and will break away from the hull, again giving rise to eddies and separation resistance.






IV.



Air resistance experienced by the above-water part of the main hull and the superstructures due to the motion of the ship through the air.


According to the book “Principles of Naval Arquitecture” (PNA), what is the correct alternative?

A) (I), (III) and (IV) solely are true

B) (I), (II) and (III) solely are true

C) All of them are true

D) (II), (III) and (IV) solely are true

E) (I) and (II) solely are true

Questão 9

According to the book “Principles of Naval Architecture” (PNA), consider the following statements regarding “Submerged Bodies” and choose the correct answer: (Chapter V - Section 1, p.2-3)


§   



 




I.



A streamlined body moving in a straight horizontal line at constant speed, deeply immersed in an unlimited ocean, presents the simplest case of resistance. Since there is no free surface, there is no wave formation and therefore no wave-making resistance. If in addition the fluid is assumed to be without viscosity (a "perfect" fluid), there will be no frictional or eddy making resistance.






II.



Near the nose, the pressure is increased above the hydrostatic pressure, along the middle of the body the pressure is decreased below it and at the stern it is again increased. The velocity distribution past the hull, by Bernoulli's Law, will be the inverse of the pressure distribution - along the midportion it will be greater than the speed of advance V and in the region of bow and stern it will be less.






III.



The velocity distribution past the hull, by Bernoulli's Law, will be the inverse of the pressure distribution - along the midportion it will be smaller than the speed of advance V and in the region of bow and stern it will be less.






IV.



Since the fluid has been assumed to be without viscosity, the pressure forces will everywhere be parallel to the hull. Over the forward part of the hull, these will have components acting towards the stern and therefore resisting the motion. Over the after part, the reverse is the case, and these components are assisting the motion. It can be shown that the resultant total forces on the fore and after bodies are equal, and the body therefore experiences no resistance.

A) (III) and (IV) solely are true

B) (I), (III) and (IV) solely are true

C) All of them are true

D) (I) and (II) solely are true

E) (I), (II) and (IV) solely are true

The pressure distribution around such a body can be determined theoretically from considerations of the potential flow. Questão 11

In a real fluid the boundary layer alters the virtual shape and length of the stern, the pressure distribution there is changed and its forward component is reduced. The pressure distribution over the forward portion is but little changed from that in a perfect fluid. According to the book “Principles of Naval Arquitecture” (PNA), there is therefore a net force on the body acting against the motion, giving rise to a resistance which is variously referred to as: (Chapter V - Section 1, p.2)

A) residuary resistance

B) frictional resistance

C) bare-hull resistance

D) form drag or viscous pressure drag

E) eddy resistance

Questão 12

(PROVA PSPP 2006) If a body submerged in an ideal (viscous fluid) as the fluid flows around it, there is a pressure distribution normal to the body. In the forward section of the hull there is a component of pressure resisting motion, and in the aft section of the body there is a component of pressure assisting motion. In an ideal fluid these pressure forces are equal and the body experiences no resistance. However, water is not an ideal fluid, and therefore some differences in the flow around the body exist. In the forward portion of the hull pressure forces act normal to the surface, however, in the aft portion of the hull the boundary layer reduces the forward component of pressure. This reduction in the forward acting component results in a net resistance force that is called: (Chapter V - Section 1, p.2)

A) Frictional resistance.

B) Wake resistance.

C) Wave making resistance.

D) Viscous pressure drag.

E) Wave-breaking resistance.

Questão 13

In a real fluid, too, the body experiences frictional resistance and perhaps eddy resistance also. The fluid immediately in contact with the surface of the body is carried along with the surface, and that in the close vicinity is set in motion in the same direction as that in which the body is moving. This results in a layer of water, which gets gradually thicker from the bow to the stern, and in which the velocity varies from that of the body at its surface to that appropriate to the potential flow pattern (almost zero for a slender body) at the outer edge of the layer. According to the book “Principles of Naval Arquitecture” (PNA), this layer is called the boundary layer, and the momentum supplied to the water in it by the hull is a measure of the: (Chapter V - Section 1, p.2-3)

A) bare-hull resistance

B) viscous pressure drag

C) frictional resistance

D) form drag

E) eddy resistance

Questão 15

Considere as afirmativas relacionadas à “Surface Ships” e responda: (Chapter V - Section 1, p.3)

§   



 




I.



A ship moving on the surface of the sea experiences frictional resistance and eddymaking, separation, and viscous pressure drag in the same way as does the submerged body. However, the presence of the free surface adds a further component. The movement of the hull through the water creates a pressure distribution similar to that around the submerged body.






II.



There are important differences in the pressure distribution over the hull of a surface ship because of the surface wave disturbance created by the ship's forward motion. There is a smaller pressure acting over the bow, as indicated by the usually prominent bow wave build-up, and the pressure increase at the stern, in and just below the free surface, is always less than around a submerged body.






III.



The resulting added resistance corresponds to the drain of energy into the wave system, which spreads out astern of the ship and has to be continuously recreated. Hence, it has been called wave-making resistance.






IV.



The result of the interference of the wave systems originating at bow, shoulders (if any) and stern is to produce a series of transverse waves spreading outwards from the ship at a relatively sharp angle to the centerline and a series of divergent waves along the hull on each side and behind in the wake.






V.



The presence of the wave systems modifies the skin friction and other resistances, and there is a very complicated interaction among all the different components.


De acordo com o contido no livro “Principles of Naval Architecture” (SNAME – 3ª edição: 1988/1989), analise as afirmativas, identifique as verdadeiras e assinale a opção correta:

A) Apenas as afirmativas (I), (II), (III) e (IV) estão corretas

C) Apenas as afirmativas (I), (III) e (V) estão corretas

D) Todas as afirmativas estão corretas

E) Apenas as afirmativas (I) e (V) estão corretas

Questão 31

Considere as afirmativas relacionadas à “STEAM TURBINE”: (Chapter VI – Sec.1, p.128-129)

§   



 




I.



The turbine is nonreversible and its rotational speed for best economy is far in excess of the most efficient rpm of usual propeller types. Despite these disadvantages it is not necessary to install separate reversing turbines.






II.



The mechanical geared drive has been used most widely up to the present. It permits the operation of engine and propeller at their most economical speedswith a power loss in the gears of only 2 to 4 percent.






III.



The reduction in RPM between turbine and propeller shaft can also be attained by electrical means. The turbine in such an installation is directly coupled to a generator, both running at the same high speed for efficient operation.






IV.



The generator supplies a motor directly mounted on the propeller shaft, driving the latter at the RPM most desirable for high propeller efficiency. This system eliminates any direct shafting between turbines and propeller, and so gives the naval architect greater freedom in laying out the general arrangement of the ship to best advantage.






V.



In twinscrew ships fitted with two sets of turboalternators, considerable economy can be achieved when using half power.






VI.



The turboelectric drive also eliminates the reversing turbine, gives great flexibility and rapidity of maneuvering, and prevents racing of the propeller. These advantages are gained, however, at the expense of rather high first cost and somewhat greater transmission losses.


De acordo com o contido no livro “Principles of Naval Architecture” (SNAME – 3ª edição: 1988/1989), analise as afirmativas, identifique as verdadeiras e assinale a opção correta:

A) Todas as afirmativas estão corretas

B) Apenas as afirmativas (II), (III), (IV), (V) e (VI) estão corretas

C) Apenas as afirmativas (I), (IV) e (VI) estão corretas

D) Apenas as afirmativas (II), (III) e (VI) estão corretas

E) Apenas as afirmativas (I), (II), (III) e (IV) estão corretas

Questão 35

Indicated power is measured in the cylinders by means of an instrument (an indicator) which records continuously the steam or gas pressure throughout the length of the piston travel. From the resultant indicator card the mean effective pressure is measured and PI is calculated for top end and bottom end separately: PI = pM . L. A. n, where: (Chapter VI – Sec.1, p.129-130)  

§   



 




I.



pM is mean effeective pressure, kN/m2.






II.



L is length of piston stroke, m.






III.



A is effective piston área, sq m.






IV.



n is number of working strokes per sec.

Questão 43

The shaft power is taken as the power delivered to the shaft by the main engines aft of the gearing and thrust block, so that the difference between PS and PD represents the power lost in friction in the shaft bearings and stern tube. The ratio PD/PS is called the shaft transmission efficiency.

The propulsive efficiency is defined as: Propulsive efficiency = quasi-propulsive coefficient times shaft transmission efficiency

Questão 44

The efficiency of an engineering operation is generally defined as the ratio of the useful work or power obtained to that expended in carrying out the operation.

In the case of a ship the useful power obtained is that used in overcoming the resistance to motion at a certain speed, which is represented by the effective power PE. The power put in to achieve this result is not so easily defined.

According to the book “Principles of Naval Architecture” (PNA), consider the following statements regarding “Propulsive Efficiency”, and choose the incorrect answer: (Chapter VI – Sec.1, p.130-131)

A) The shaft power is taken as the power delivered to the shaft by the main engines aft of the gearing and thrust block, so that the difference between PS and PD represents the power lost in friction in the shaft bearings and stern tube. The ratio PD/PS is called the shaft transmission efficiency. The propulsive efficiency is defined as: Propulsive efficiency = quasi-propulsive coefficient times shaft transmission efficiency

B) Since mechanical efficiencies, gear losses and shaft-transmission losses all vary from ship to ship, according to the type of machinery and general layout, and even in a given ship with the load at which the machinery is operating at a particular time, it is difficult to define the hydrodynamic efficiency of a hull-propeller combination in terms of such an overall propulsive efficiency. A much more meaningful measure of efficiency of propulsion is the ratio of the useful power obtained, PE , to the power actually delivered to the propeller, PD (quasi-propulsive coeficiente).

C) The shaft transmission loss is usually taken as about 3 percent for ships with machinery aft and 2 percent for those with machinery amidships, but no very exact information exists on this point.

D) In a ship with reciprocating engines, it can be measured by the power developed in the cylinders themselves as given by the indicated power, PI. The overall propulsive efficiency in this case would be expressed by the ratio PE/PI.  In the case of turbines it is usual to measure the power in terms of the shaft power delivered to the shafting abaft the gearing, and the overall propulsive efficiency is PE/PS..

E) When using the power measured by torsionmeter, the answer will depend on the position of the meter along the shaft. To approach as closely as possible to the power delivered to the propeller, it should be as near to the stern tube as circumstances permit. It is often assumed that ɳS = 1.0.

Questão 46

Controllability encompasses all aspects of regulating a ship's trajectory, speed, and orientation at sea as well as in restricted waters where positioning and station keeping are of particular concern. Controllability includes starting, steering a steady course, turning, slowing, stopping, backing, and in the case of submarines, diving.

Performance varies with water, depth, channel restrictions, and hydrodynamic interference from nearby vessels or obstacles.

Questão 54

In the marine world, interaction is caused when the pressure fields around ships interact. All ships are in a state of balance in the water, held in position at rest by hydrostatic pressures to which are added dynamic pressures when the ship begins to move. Dynamic pressures are those which cause (and indeed are represented by) the familiar diverging and transverse wave systems generated by a ship moving calm water.

If the pressure system acting over the underwater hull of the ship is changed in any way, its state of balance will be affected. It may go faster or slower, move off course, sink deeper into the water or rise on to its surface (squat), or possibly trim differently.

In shallow water, ship-ship interaction becomes more severe from these purely hydrodynamic causes.

According to the book “Squat Interaction Manoeuvring” (The Nautical Institute, Ed.: 1995), model tests have shown that, in general terms, ship-ship interaction varies: as the square of the speed; inversely with distance off; and, roughly as the inverse square root of the underkeel clearance to draught ratio. These mean that: (PAGE 2-3)

A) the slower the ship moves, the better interaction becomes. the smaller the lateral separation between ships, the better. the greater the underkeel clearance, the bigger the effect.

B) the slower the ship moves, the worse interaction becomes. the smaller the lateral separation between ships, the better. the greater the underkeel clearance, the bigger the effect.

C) the faster the ship moves, the worse interaction becomes. the greater the lateral separation between ships, the better. the smaller the underkeel clearance, the smaller the effect.

D) the faster the ship moves, the better interaction becomes. the greater the lateral separation between ships, the better. the greater the underkeel clearance, the bigger the effect.

E) the faster the ship moves, the worse interaction becomes. the greater the lateral separation between ships, the better. the smaller the underkeel clearance, the bigger the effect.

Questão 55

Passing on reciprocal courses has the merit (from an interaction point of view) of being quick so that often the ship does not have time to react to the various interaction forces and moments it feels.

According to the book “Squat Interaction Manoeuvring” (The Nautical Institute, Ed.: 1995), consider the following statements regarding “head-on passing” and choose the correct statement: (Page 3)

A) Interaction begins to be felt with the bows of both ships being pushed away from each other accompanied by a slight reduction in speed. At the same time, the vessels feel a slight bodily repulsion. As the ships pass, the "bow-out" moment turns to "bow-in" and the repulsion reduces. The "bow-out" moment then returns as passing continues, but is now stronger. Indeed it may cause both ships to sheer away from each other once they have passed. A slight increase in speed may also be felt. Finally a weak "bow-in" moment accompanied by a repulsion may be felt.

B) Interaction begins to be felt with the bows of both ships being pushed away from each other accompanied by a slight increase in speed. At the same time, the vessels feel a slight bodily repulsion. As the ships pass, the "bow-out" moment turns to "bow-in" and the repulsion reduces. The "bow-out" moment then returns as passing continues, but is now stronger. Indeed it may cause both ships to sheer away from each other once they have passed. A slight reduction in speed may also be felt. Finally a weak "bow-in" moment accompanied by a repulsion may be felt.

C) Interaction begins to be felt with the bows of both ships being pushed away from each other accompanied by a slight reduction in speed. At the same time, the vessels feel a slight bodily repulsion. As the ships pass, the "bow-in" moment turns to "bow-out" and the repulsion reduces. The "bow-out" moment then returns as passing continues, but is now stronger. Indeed it may cause both ships to sheer away from each other once they have passed. A slight reduction in speed may also be felt. Finally a weak "bow-in" moment accompanied by a repulsion may be felt.

D) Interaction begins to be felt with the bows of both ships being pushed away from each other accompanied by a slight increase in speed. At the same time, the vessels feel a slight bodily repulsion. As the ships pass, the "bow-out" moment turns to "bow-in" and the repulsion increase. The "bow-out" moment then returns as passing continues, but is now stronger. Indeed it may cause both ships to sheer away from each other once they have passed. A slight reduction in speed may also be felt. Finally a strong "bow-in" moment accompanied by a repulsion may be felt.

E) Interaction begins to be felt with the bows of both ships being pushed away from each other accompanied by a slight reduction in speed. At the same time, the vessels feel a slight bodily attraction. As the ships pass, the "bow-out" moment turns to "bow-in" and the repulsion reduces. The "bow-out" moment then returns as passing continues, but is now weaker. Indeed it may cause both ships to sheer away from each other once they have passed. A slight reduction in speed may also be felt. Finally a weak "bow-in" moment accompanied by a repulsion may be felt.

Questão 61

(PROVA PSPP 2011) De acordo com o contido no “SQUAT INTERACTION MANEOUVERING” (The Nautical Institute, edição: 1995), analise as afirmativas abaixo, identifique quais são verdadeiras e assinale a opção correta: (Page 3-5)

§   



 




I)



Navegando em um canal estreito, um navio, após passar por outro, deixando-o por BB, com a margem mais próxima a BE, (“head-on passing”), tende a ser jogado para BE, podendo colidir com a margem a BE caso não antecipe uma manobra de correção de atitude do navio.






II)



Navegando em um canal estreito, um navio, após passar por outro, deixando-o por BB, com a margem mais próxima a BE, (“head-on passing”), tende a ser jogado para BB, podendo colidir com a margem a BB caso não antecipe uma manobra de correção de atitude do navio.






III)



Navegando em um canal estreito, um navio, após passar por outro, deixando-o por BB, com a margem mais próxima a BE, (“head-on passing”), tende a manter o rumo sem aproximar-se da margem mais próxima.






IV)

V)

§   



 




I)



Em águas rasas, a interação navio-navio torna-se mais severa do que aquela originada puramente por causas hidrodinâmicas em águas profundas.






II)



Navegando em um canal, junto à margem por BE, a interação sofrida pelo navio devido aos chamados “bank effects” pode ser, em princípio, contrabalançada carregando o leme para uma posição adequada, de modo a manter o navio guinando para BE.






III)



Em uma manobra de “head-on passing”, um navio navegando em um canal estreito sofre um pequeno aumento de velocidade no início da interação com outro navio, podendo ocorrer uma leve redução da sua velocidade no final da passagem.






IV)



Um rebocador com propulsão convencional, quando operando ao longo de um navio de grande porte, sofre mudanças repentinas nos sentidos das forças e momentos gerados pela interação com o navio, à medida que ele altera sua posição em relação à popa e à proa do navio.






V)



O efeito “squat” sofrido por um navio pode ser medido através da diferença das leituras entre os calados na proa com o navio em movimento adiante e com o navio parado.

A) Apenas as afirmativas (I), (II) e (IV) são verdadeiras.

B) Apenas as afirmativas (I), (II), (III) e (IV) são verdadeiras.

C) Apenas as afirmativas (II), (III) e (V) são verdadeiras.

D) Apenas as afirmativas (I), (III), (IV) e (V) são verdadeiras.

E) Apenas as afirmativas (III), (IV) e (V) são verdadeiras.

Questão 83

A largura de influência 'FB' (width of influence) é uma largura artificial de água usada em cálculos de squat. É usado para navios que operam em águas abertas (open water conditions). Depende do tipo de navio que está sendo considerado.

Utilizando esta largura de influência, pode-se colocar o navio em um canal artificial e então prosseguir, calculando o squat máximo como se a embarcação estivesse operando em um rio ou canal.

De acordo com o contido no “Squat Interaction Manoeuvring” (The Nautical Institute, edição: 1995), considere as seguintes afirmativas, identifique a verdadeira e assinale a alternativa correta: (Página 25)

A) Qualquer largura de canal maior que 'FB' dará valores idênticos de squat. É por isso que é chamada de “largura de influência”. Qualquer largura de canal menor que 'FB' produzirá maior resistência, perda de velocidade e de RPM e aumento de squat.

B) Qualquer largura de canal menor que 'FB' dará valores menores de squat. É por isso que é chamada de “largura de influência”. Qualquer largura de canal maior que 'FB' produzirá maior resistência, perda de velocidade e de RPM e aumento no squat.

C) Qualquer largura de canal maior que 'FB' dará valores menores de squat. É por isso que é chamada de “largura de influência”. Qualquer largura de canal menor que 'FB' produzirá maior resistência, perda de velocidade e de RPM e diminuição de squat.

D) Qualquer largura de canal menor que 'FB' dará valores idênticos de squat. É por isso que é chamada de “largura de influência”. Qualquer largura de canal maior que 'FB' produzirá menor resistência, ganho de velocidade, perda de RPM e diminuição de squat.

E) Qualquer largura de canal maior que 'FB' dará valores maiores de squat. É por isso que é chamada de “largura de influência”. Qualquer largura de canal menor que 'FB' produzirá maior resistência, perda de velocidade e de RPM e aumento de squat.

E) A maneira mais eficiente de prevenir o squat excessivo e seus efeitos é reduzir a velocidade. A outra forma é aumentar o valor H/T, que pode ser conseguido descarregando a carga dentro do navio, como água de lastro, ou movendo o navio para águas mais profundas.

Questão 92

What is “transverse squat” on container vessels and modern supertankers?

When the amidships of both vessels are in line with each other across the river the squats of both vessels when moving ahead can be increased by 50% to 100% compared to a single ship moving ahead up the river.

According to the book “Squat Interaction Manoeuvring” (The Nautical Institute, Ed.: 1995), consider the following statements and choose the correct answer: (Page 33-33/w)

A) In the situation described above, both vessels will move towards each other accompanied by an angle of heel. The bilge plating of both vessels nearest to the river banks will move nearer to the river bed. This is “transverse squat”, that is, gain or increase of underkeel clearance. Too much “transverse squat” causes grounding and damage of the bilge keel and/or bilge keel plating strakes.

B) In the situation described above, both vessels will move away from each other accompanied by an angle of heel. The bilge plating of both vessels further along to the river banks will move farther to the river bed. This is “transverse squat”, that is, gain or increase of underkeel clearance. Too much “transverse squat” causes grounding and damage of the bilge keel and/or bilge keel plating strakes.

C) In the situation described above, both vessels will move towards each other accompanied by an angle of heel. The bilge plating of both vessels nearest to the river banks will move nearer to the river bed. This is “transverse squat”, that is, loss or decrease of underkeel clearance. Too much “transverse squat” causes grounding and damage of the bilge keel and/or bilge keel plating strakes.

D) In the situation described above, both vessels will move away from each other accompanied by an angle of heel. The bilge plating of both vessels nearest to the river banks will move nearer to the river bed. This is “transverse squat”, that is, loss or decrease of underkeel clearance. Too much “transverse squat” causes grounding and damage of the bilge keel and/or bilge keel plating strakes.

E) In the situation described above, both vessels will move towards each other accompanied by an angle of heel. The bilge plating of both vessels further along to the river banks will move farther to the river bed. This is “transverse squat”, that is, loss or decrease of underkeel clearance. Too much “transverse squat” causes grounding and damage of the bilge keel and/or bilge keel plating strakes.

Questão 102

O passo de um propulsor é o mesmo movendo para vante e a ré. A principal diferença, quando a ré, é que a seção transversal da pá (blade cross-section) agora é invertida daquela mais adequada para evitar cavitação e separação.

De acordo com o contido no livro “Naval Shiphandling” (Crenshaw, Russel Sidnor, Jr - 4ª edição: 1975), considere as afirmativas abaixo, identifique a verdadeira e assinale a opção correta: (Page 19)

A) Quando operando à ré, um determinado hélice é menos eficiente do que quando girando a vante. Isso significa que menos potência será necessária para uma determinada rpm a ré do que para uma mesma rpm a vante, e assim, uma tração bem maior será gerada quando girando a ré.

B) Quando operando à ré, um determinado hélice é menos eficiente do que quando girando a vante. Isso significa que mais potência será necessária para uma determinada rpm a ré do que para uma mesma rpm a vante, e assim, uma tração bem menor será gerada quando girando a ré.

C) Quando operando a ré, um determinado propulsor é menos eficiente do que quando girando a vante. Isso significa que mais potência será necessária para uma determinada rpm a ré do que para uma mesma rpm a vante, mas aproximadamente a mesma tração será gerada para essa rpm, seja girando a vante ou a ré.

D) Quando operando a ré, um determinado propulsor é tão eficiente do que quando girando a vante. Isso significa que a mesma potência será necessária para uma determinada rpm a ré do que para uma mesma rpm a vante e, aproximadamente a mesma tração será gerada para essa rpm, seja girando a vante ou a ré.

E) Quando operando a ré, um determinado propulsor é mais eficiente do que quando girando a vante. Isso significa que menos potência será necessária para uma determinada rpm a ré do que para uma mesma rpm a vante, mas aproximadamente a mesma tração será gerada para essa rpm, seja girando a vante ou a ré.

Questão 103

One of the most important sources of force on a ship is her own propeller. One would expect, since the propeller is designed to propel the ship, that turning the propeller AHEAD would cause the ship to move straight ahead, and that turning the propeller ASTERN would cause the ship to move straight astern. This is not necessarily the case, however, and the shiphandler must study the action of a propeller in order to be able to predict its action on his ship.

According to the book “Naval Shiphandling” (4th ed.: 1975), consider the following statements regarding “The propeller” and choose the incorrect answer: (Page 18-19)

A) Though the propeller and shaft are well supported to withstand large forces along the axis of the shaft, the length of external shafting and the small number of relatively weak supports (the struts) form a structure poorly designed to withstand large radial forces at the propeller.

B) With a well-designed propeller the speed varies nearly linearly with shaft rpm. This relation holds true until a speed is reached where separation and cavitation become pronounced.

C) When operating astern, a given propeller is less efficient than when going ahead. This means that more power will be required for a given shaft rpm astern than for the same rpm ahead, but approximately the same thrust will result from a given rpm whether turning ahead or astern.

D) A propeller is designed for a given ahead speed, but it does not work quite well turning astern. The pitch of the propeller is the same going astern as it is going ahead, the major difference being that the blade cross-section is now reversed from that best suited for preventing cavitation and turbulence.

E) A fixed blade propeller is designed for optimum performance at one particular speed of the ship, usually the maximum speed, but it can be depended upon to operate efficiently at all normal speeds.

Questão 104

Close to the hull, the velocity of the water relative to the ship is very small; that is, the water is being carried along with the ship. At some distance out from the hull, the relative velocity approaches the velocity of the ship. We can take some arbitrary point as the limit of the boundary layer and can thus examine the thickness of the frictronal wake. A) 5 percent of the ship’s speed

B) 1 percent of the ship’s speed

In a single propeller ship going ahead, with a right-hand screw, as a blade moves downward it meets water which is moving upward as well as aft. According to R. S. Crenshaw, Jr., in the book “Naval Shiphandling”, this is equivalent: (Page 23)

A) to increase the relative velocity and the angle of attack at same time and thus, decrease in thrust is experienced. On the opposite side, an increase in thrust is experienced. The net effect of the reaction to the inclined flow, then, is a thrust tending to turn the ship to the left.

B) to increase the relative velocity and the angle of attack at same time and thus, increase in thrust is experienced. On the opposite side, a decrease in thrust is experienced. The net effect of the reaction to the inclined flow, then, is a torque tending to twist the ship to the left.

C) to decrease the relative velocity and the angle of attack at same time and thus, decrease in thrust is experienced. On the opposite side, an increase in thrust is experienced. The net effect of the reaction to the inclined flow, then, is a torque tending to twist the ship to the left.

D) to decrease the relative velocity and the angle of attack at same time and thus, decrease in thrust is experienced. On the opposite side, an increase in thrust is experienced. The net effect of the reaction to the inclined flow, then, is a thrust tending to turn the ship to the right.

Questão 110

“The axis of the propeller shaft is inclined to the axis of water flow past the propeller because of the inward and upward movement of the water under the stern. The inward movement is symmetrical on both sides of the stern and can be neglected but the upward movement produces a marked effect. As a blade moves downward to its horizontal position, it meets water which is moving upward as well as aft. This is equivalent to increasing the relative velocity and the angle of attack at the same time, and thus an increase in thrust is experienced. On the opposite side the port side for a righthand screw, a decrease in thrust is experienced.”


De acordo com o contido no livro “Naval Shiphandling” (Crenshaw, Russel Sidnor, Jr - 4ª edição: 1975), a afirmativa acima se refere ao: (SIDE FORCE FROM A SINGLE SCREW - Page 23)

A) Inclination effect

B) Shallow submergence effect

C) Helical discharge effect

D) Following submergence effect

E) Following wake effect

§   



 




I.



The following wake effect, then, is to produce a net force tending to move the stern to starboard and cause the ship to veer to the left.






II.



The net effect of the reaction to the inclined flow, then, is a torque tending to twist the ship to the left.






III.



The net effect of the helical discharge, then, is to tend to turn the ship to the left. (This effect can be altered or increased by an un­symmetrical rudder, especially if the rudder does not extend across the entire disc).






IV.



The shallow submergence effect, then, is to tend to move the stern to port and cause the ship to veer to the right.

Questão 114

So, a pilot uses forces of engines, propellers, rudders, the wind and the tide, lines, tugs and anchors to control the velocities of a ship. One of the most important sources of force on a ship is her own propeller and the pilot must understand the action of a propeller in order to be able to predict its action on ship. §

The following wake effect produces a net force tending to move the stern to right and cause the ship to veer to the port.






II)



The inclination effect is net effect that tends to twist the ship to the right.






III)



The shallow submergence effect tends the stern to starboard and cause the ship to veer to the left.






IV)



The helical discharge effect tends to turn the ship to the right.

Questão 117

(PROVA PSPP 2012) De acordo com o contido sobre esforços hidrodinâmicos sobre um navio em movimento no livro “Naval Shiphandling” (Crenshaw, Russel Sidnor, Jr – 4ª edição: 1975), analise as afirmativas abaixo, identifique as verdadeiras e assinale a opção correta: (Pages 15-25)

§   



 




I)



A diferença de pressão resultante causada pelo movimento da água é proporcional à densidade da água e ao quadrado da velocidade do movimento. (pág.15)






II)



Em um navio com dois eixos propulsores instalados, com propulsores idênticos girando em sentidos opostos para movimentar o navio adiante ou a ré, a força lateral exercida sobre o navio devido à ação dos propulsores é o dobro da força lateral que ocorreria caso o navio fosse dotado com apenas um desses propulsores. (pág.25)






III)



A velocidade do ar deve ser aproximadamente 30 vezes a velocidade da água, para que a pressão dinâmica resultante da ação dos movimentos do ar e da água, sobre um mesmo corpo e aplicada sob as mesmas condições, seja a mesma. (pág.15)






IV)



Considerando-se um hidrofólio como sendo um corpo projetado para obter uma força de sustentação quando inclinado em relação à direção do fluxo da água, a força de arrasto (drag) é definida como sendo a componente da força exercida nesse corpo que atua na direção paralela ao fluxo de corrente livre relativo (relative free stream flow) da água. (pág.16)






V)



Um submarino dotado com apenas um propulsor, quando navegando isolado em grande profundidade e em mar aberto, não sofre ação apreciável, na sua popa, da força lateral gerada pela rotação do propulsor. (pág.20)

E) Apenas as afirmativas (I), (II), (III) e (IV) são verdadeiras.

With twin screws, then, the side forces are large and are uniformly in the direction indicated by the direction of rotation of the propeller.

When moving steadily ahead, the force to starboard on a right-hand screw is not appreciably diminished by following wake effect, so we can expect strong side force to be present even though normally cancelled by the side force from the opposite screw.

Questão 121

In all types of conventional ships, a side force is experienced whenever the propellers are rotated. Though the magnitude of the force may vary with the type of ship, the direction is nearly always that indicated by the direction of rotation of the propellers (as trough the blades were bearing against a more solid layer during the lower part of their travel). If the Shiphandler has considered the origin of these forces, he will know what reaction to expect under any given set of conditions. For a certain ship, a little experimentation wiII indicate the magnitude and character of the side forces that can be expected and the conning officer can turn these forces to good use in handling his ship.

According to the book “Naval Shiphandling” (4th ed.: 1975), consider the following statements regarding “side forces with twin-screws” and choose the incorrect answer: (Page 26-28)

A) In a twin-rudder ship, the helical discharge effect is not only present but is greatly augmented by the shape of the rudder.

B) If the ship has a single rudder, it will probably not be within the region of helical discharge current so this effect will be absent in such ships.

C) The following wake effect is considerably increased with normal twin-screw design.

D) The inclination effect is present in all types of ships.

E) The shallow submergence effect is less common with twin-screw design, especially with warships, since it is most unusual that a propeller tip will break surface.

Questão 122

In the normal twin-screw installation, the propellers turn in opposite directions when driving ahead or astern, and the side forces then cancel. To increase the maneuverability of these ships, it has become the convention to allow the side force to augment the moment resulting from the shafts being offset from the centerline.

Thus we find the propellers turning so that the blade tips are moving outboard during the upper half of their travel when driving ahead. This calls for a righthand screw on the starboard shaft and a lefthand screw on the port shaft. To determine the magnitude of the side force to be expected in a twin-screw ship, we should consider the character of the flow in the vicinity of the propellers.

According to the book “Naval Shiphandling” (4th ed.: 1975), consider the following statements regarding “SIDE FORCE WITH TWIN SCREW”, and choose the incorrect answer: (Page 26-28)

A) Since the only opposing effect - the shallow submergence effect - has been diminished in twin-screw design, the side force on a righthand screw turning ahead is definitely to starboard, and the side force for a lefthand screw turning ahead is definitely to port.

B) If the ship has a single rudder, it will probably not be within the region of helical discharge current so this effect will be absent in such ships. In a twin-rudder ship, the helical discharge effect is not only present but is greatly augmented by the shape of the rudder.

C) The inclination of the shaft axis to the direction of flow is just as marked with twin screws as with single. Not only is the upward motion of the wake still present, but, the inclination of the shaft is downward. Since the engine is inside the hull and hence the shaft must pass throuqh the bottom to reach the propeller in any type of ship, the inclination effect is present in all types of ships.

D) The blade tips as they pass closest to the hull are working in a following wake of only 15 percent of ship's speed, and throughout the majority of the propeller disc there is no appreciable following wake. Consequently, the following wake effect is considerably reduced with normal twin-screw design.

E) The shallow submergence effect is less common with twin-screw design, especially with warships, since it is most unusual that a propeller tip will break surface. The churning and air drawing is present, however, so this effect is stiII to be considered.

Questão 136

In addition to the general sources of resistance which the ship encounters in smooth water, there is additional resistance if the surface of the water is not smooth. As the ship encounters seas from ahead, her trim is constantly changing and she crashes into the approaching seas. Because of Froude's close association with the concept of speed-length ratio, the parameter VK / is called the Froude number, with the symbol Fn.

 




III.



Quando VK é expressa em nós, L em pés, e g em pés/s², a relação entre V/ and Froude number é: Fn = 0,298 VK / ou VK / = 3,355 Fn.

C) Apenas as afirmativas (II) e (III) são corretas

D) Todas as afirmativas são corretas

E) Apenas as afirmativas (I) e (II) são corretas

Questão 148

Considere as afirmativas relacionadas ao termo p/ ρ.V² e responda: (Chapter V - Section 2, p.6)

§   



 




I.



If the atmospheric pressure above the water surface is ignored and p refers only to the water head, then for corresponding points in model and ship p will vary directly with the speed length ratio.
 




II.



At corresponding speeds V2 varies with λ in the same way so that p/ρ.V2 will be the same for model and ship. Since the atmospheric pressure is usually the same in model and ship, when it is included in p, so that the latter is the total pressure at a given point, the value of p/ρ.V2 will be much greater for model than for ship.






III.



Fortunately, most of the hydrodynamic forces arise from differences in local pressures, and these are proportional to V2, so that the forces are not affected by the atmospheric pressure so long as the fluid remains in contact with the model and ship surfaces. When the pressure approaches very low values, however, the water is unable to follow surfaces where there is some curvature and cavities form in the water, giving rise to cavitation. The similarity conditions are then no longer fulfilled.






IV.



Since the absolute or total pressure is greater in the model than in the ship, the former gives no warning of such behavior. For tests in which this danger is known to be present, special facilities have been devised, such as variable-pressure water tunnels, channels or towing basins, where the correctly scaled-down total pressure can be attained at the same time that the Froude condition is met.

B) Apenas as afirmativas (II), (III) e (IV) são verdadeiras.

C) Apenas as afirmativas (I) e (II) são verdadeiras.

D) Apenas as afirmativas (II) e (III) são verdadeiras.

E) Apenas as afirmativas (I) e (IV) são verdadeiras.

Questão 153

Considere as seguintes afirmativas: (Chapter V - Section 2, p.6-7)

§   



 




I.



The conditions of mechanical similitude for both friction and wave-making can be satisfied in a single test.
 




II.



in order to run the model at the correct wavemaking corresponding speed, and yet keep the value of V.L/ ν  the same for both model and ship, a fluid would have to be found for use with the model which had a kinematic viscosity coefficient only 1/125 that of water. No such fluid is known. In wind-tunnel work, similitude can be attained by using compressed air in the model tests, so decreasing v and increasing VL/v to the required value.
 




III.



The practical method of overcoming this fundamental difficulty in the use of ship models is to deal with the frictional and the wave-making resistances separately, by writing CT = CR + CF.
 




IV.



Froude reconheceu essa necessidade e, assim, tornou o teste de modelo de navio uma ferramenta prática. Percebeu que a resistência friccional e a resistência residual obedecem à mesma lei, embora desconhecesse a relação expressa pela equação CF = RF / ½. ρ. S. V2 = f (V.L/ ν).

Questão 162

In order to apply the results to ships, the derived skin-friction coefficients had to be extrapolated to much greater lengths and speeds. W. Froude did not give these extrapolated figures in his reports, but suggested two methods which might be used for their derivation. According to the book “Principles of Naval Arquitecture” (PNA), consider the following statements regarding to “Froud’s Experiments on Friction” and mark true (T) or false (F): (Chapter V - Section 3, p.8)

§   



 




(   )



In his own words, "it is at once seen that, at a length of 50 feet, the decrease, with increasing length, of the friction per square foot of every additional length is so small that it will make no very great difference in our estimate of the total resistance of a surface 300 ft long whether we assume such decrease to continue at the same rate throughout the last 250 feet of the surface, or to cease entirely after 50 feet; while it is perfectly certain that the truth must lie somewhere between these assumptions."






(   )



Froude pointed out that the additional resistance could be accounted for by assuming that the copper-sheathed hull was equivalent to smooth varnish over 2/3 of the wetted surface and to calico over the rest. This he considered reasonable, and the two resistance curves were then almost identical, which he took as a visible demonstration of the correctness of his law of comparison.






(   )



In his paper on the Greyhound trials, Froude states quite clearly how he applied his idea of the "equivalent plank" resistance: "For this calculation the immersed skin was carefully measured, and the resistance due to it determined upon the hypothesis that it is equivalent to that of a rectangular surface of equal area, and of length (in the line of motion) equal to that of the model, moving at the same speed."






(   )



The 1876 values of frictional coefficients were stated to apply to new, clean, freshly painted steel surfaces, but they lie considerably below those now generally accepted for smooth surfaces. Nevertheless, they are still used today in some towing tanks.

B) (V) (F) (V) (F)

C) (F) (F) (V) (V)

D) (V) (V) (V) (F)

E) (F) (V) (F) (V)

Questão 163

Considere as seguintes afirmativas relacionadas à “Two-dimensional Frictional Resistance Formulations”: (Chapter V - Section 3, p.8-9)

§   



 




I.



Osborne Reynolds fez a água fluir através de um tubo de vidro, introduzindo uma fina corrente de tinta na linha central na entrada do tubo. Quando a velocidade era pequena, o corante permanecia como um filamento reto paralelo ao eixo do tubo.






II.



A certa velocidade, que Reynolds chamou de velocidade crítica VC, o filamento começou a tremular, tornou-se sinuoso e finalmente perdeu toda a definição do contorno, o corante enchendo todo o tubo.






III.



A resistência experimentada pelo fluido ao longo de um determinado comprimento de tubo foi medida encontrando-se a perda de velocidade. Vários diâmetros do tubo, D, foram usados, e a viscosidade cinemática foi variada pelo aquecimento da água.






IV.



Reynolds descobriu que as leis de resistência correspondiam exatamente às velocidades na proporção ν/D, e quando os resultados eram plotados aritmeticamente: VC = 2000.ν/D.
 




V.



Abaixo da velocidade crítica, a resistência ao fluxo no tubo variava diretamente com a velocidade, enquanto que, para velocidades mais altas, variava com uma potência da velocidade um pouco menor que 2. Quando a relação anterior é escrita na forma VC.D/ ν = 2000, a semelhança com a Equação CF = RF / (½.ρ.S.V2) = f(V.L/ ν) é óbvia.

Questão 171

Neste experimento, Osborne Reynolds fez a água fluir através de um tubo de vidro, introduzindo uma fina corrente de corante na linha central da entrada do tubo. Quando a velocidade era pequena, o corante permanecia como um filamento reto paralelo ao eixo do tubo. A uma certa velocidade, que Reynolds chamou de velocidade crítica VC , o filamento começou a oscilar, tornou-se sinuoso e finalmente perdeu toda a definição de contorno.

Experimentos como os realizados por Reynolds sugeriram que havia dois regimes de fluxo separados possíveis, cada um associado a uma lei de resistência diferente: laminar; e turbulento.

A) A transição do fluxo laminar para o fluxo turbulento não ocorre simultaneamente em toda a placa. A transição começa quando o número de Reynolds atinge um valor crítico RC. À medida que a velocidade V aumenta além deste valor, o ponto de transição se move para vante de modo que o valor local do número de Reynolds, Vx/v , permaneça igual ao valor crítico, sendo x a distância do ponto de transição do bordo de ataque da placa.

B) A transição do fluxo laminar para o fluxo turbulento ocorre simultaneamente em toda a placa. A transição começa quando o número de Reynolds atinge um valor crítico RC. À medida que a velocidade V aumenta além deste valor, o ponto de transição se move para ré de modo que o valor local do número de Reynolds, Vx/v , permaneça igual ao valor crítico, sendo x a distância do ponto de transição do bordo de fuga da placa.

À medida que a velocidade V aumenta além deste valor, o ponto de transição se move para ré de modo que o valor local do número de Reynolds, Vx/v , permaneça igual ao valor crítico, sendo x a distância do ponto de transição do bordo de ataque da placa.

À medida que a velocidade V aumenta além deste valor, o ponto de transição se move para ré de modo que o valor local do número de Reynolds, Vx/v , permaneça igual ao valor crítico, sendo x a distância do ponto de transição do bordo de fuga da placa.

À medida que a velocidade V aumenta além deste valor, o ponto de transição se move para vante de modo que o valor local do número de Reynolds, Vx/v , permaneça igual ao valor crítico, sendo x a distância do ponto de transição do bordo de ataque da placa.

Questão 172

In this experiment, Osborne Reynolds made water flow through a glass tube, introducing a thin stream of dye on the centerline at the entrance to the tube. When the velocity was small, the dye remained as a straight filament parallel to the axis of the tube. At a certain velocity, which Reynolds called the critical velocity VC the filamento began to waver, became sinuous and finally lost all definiteness of outline, the dye filling the whole tube.

Experiments such as those performed by Reynolds suggested that there were two separate flow regimes possible, each associated with a different resistance law: laminar; and turbulent.

According to the book “Principles of Naval Architecture” (PNA), which answer best fill in the blanks? (Chapter V - Section 3, p.10)

 
“The transition from laminar flow to turbulent flow does not occur simultaneously over the whole plank. Transition begins when the Reynolds number reaches a critical value RC. As the velocity V increases beyond this value, the transition point moves forward so that the local value of the Reynolds number, Vx/v, remains equal to the critical value, x being the distance of the transition point from the leading edge of the plank. This is called the ‘_____ Reynolds number,’ and for the constant value of this local Rn at which transition takes place, x will _____ as V _____, and more and more of the plank surface will be in _____ flow and so experience a higher resistance.”

A) critical - increase - decreases - turbulent

B) critical - decrease - increases - laminar

C) critical - increase - increases - turbulent

D) local - decrease - decreases - laminar

E) local - decrease - increases - turbulent

Questão 173

The transition from laminar flow to turbulent flow does not occur simultaneously over the whole plank. Transition begins when the Reynolds number reaches a critical value RC. As the velocity V increases beyond this value, the transition point moves forward so that the local value of the Reynolds number, Vx/v, remains equal to the critical value, x being the distance of the transition point from the leading edge of the plank. This is called the "local Reynolds number," and for the constant value of this local Rn at which transition takes place, x will decrease as V increases, and more and more of the plank surface will be in turbulent flow and so experience a higher resistance. The value of CF will thus increase along a transition line and finally approach the turbulent line asymptotically. It should be noted that there is no unique transition line, the actual one followed in a given case depending upon: (Chapter V - Section 3, p.10)


§   



 




I.



the initial state of turbulence in the fluid






II.



the character of the plank surface






III.



the wetted surface area






IV.



the shape of the leading edge






V.



the aspect ratio


According to the book “Principles of Naval Arquitecture” (PNA), what is the correct alternative?

A) (I), (III) and (V) solely are true

B) All of them are true

C) (II) and (IV) solely are true

D) (I), (II) and (IV) solely are true

E) (I), (II), (IV) and (V) solely are true

Questão 174

A transição do fluxo laminar para o fluxo turbulento não ocorre simultaneamente em toda a placa. A transição começa quando o número de Reynolds atinge um valor crítico RC. À medida que a velocidade V aumenta além deste valor, o ponto de transição se move para vante de modo que o valor local do número de Reynolds, Vx / v, permaneça igual ao valor crítico, sendo x a distância do ponto de transição da borda principal da placa. Isso é chamado de "número de Reynolds local", e para o valor constante deste Rn local no qual a transição ocorre, x irá diminuir conforme V aumenta, e mais e mais da superfície da placa estará em fluxo turbulento e assim experimentar uma maior resistência. O valor de CF irá, assim, aumentar ao longo de uma linha de transição e, finalmente, aproximar-se assintoticamente da linha turbulenta. De acordo com o contido no livro “Principles of Naval Architecture” (SNAME – 3ª edição: 1988/1989), deve-se notar que não existe uma linha de transição única, sendo a verdadeira a ser seguida em um dado caso, dependendo dos seguintes fatores, exceto: (Chapter V - Section 3, p.10)

A) the initial state of turbulence in the fluid

B) the aspect ratio

C) the shape of the leading edge

D) the character of the plank surface

E) the overall plank resistance

Questão 179

Observe a figura abaixo, considere as afirmativas relacionadas à “Three-Dimensional Viscous Resistance Formulations” e responda: (Chapter V - Section 3, p.14-15)




§   



 




I.



In association with his two-dimensional line, Hughes proposed a new method of extrapolation from model to ship. He assumed that the total model resistance coefficient CTM could be divided into two parts, CVM and CWM representing the viscous and wavemaking resistance, respectively.






II.



At low Froude numbers, CWM will become very small, and at a point where wavemaking can be neglected, the curve of CTM will become approximately parallel to the two-dimensional friction line. Hughes called this point the separation point.






III.



The value of CTM at this point can then be identified with the total viscous resistance coefficient CVM at the same point Rn0.
 




IV.



The form resistance coefficient, due at least in part to the curvature of the hull, is defined by 1 + k = CTM (Rn0) / CF0 (Rn0).
 




V.



The form factor (1 + k) is assumed to be variant with Rn and the line (1 + k) CF0 is now taken as the extrapolator for the hull form concerned, and the ship curve of CTS can be drawn above the (1 + k) CF0 curve at the appropriate values of the Reynolds number.

Questão 180

In association with his two-dimensional line, Hughes proposed a new method of extrapolation from model to ship. He assumed that the total model resistance coefficient CTM could be divided into two parts, CVM and CWM representing the viscous and wavemaking resistance, respectively. According to the book “Principles of Naval Arquitecture” (PNA), consider the following statements regarding to “Three-Dimensional Viscous Resistance Formulations” and choose the incorrect alternative: (Chapter V - Section 3, p.14-15)

A) In the Froude method the whole of the model residuary resistance coefficient CR is transferred to the ship unchanged, while in the form factor method only that part of CR attributed to viscous effects (CFORMM) is reduced in the transfer. Accordingly, the three-dimensional method gives substantially lower ship predictions and so calls for larger values of the correlation allowance CA. This procedure avoids the negative allowances sometimes found when using the Fronde method.

B) It should also be noted that in the case of the Fronde method only the slope of the two-dimensional friction line matters while in the case of the form factor approach the vertical position of the line also affects the ship prediction. The choice of the basic line becomes an essential factor in the case of the three-dimensional approach.

C) At high Froude numbers, CWM will become very small, and at a point where wavemaking can be neglected, the curve of CTM Will become approximately parallel to the two-dimensional friction line. Hughes called this point the run-in point.

D) The form factor (1 + k) is assumed to be invariant with Rn and the line (1 + k) CF0 is now taken as the extrapolator for the hull form concerned, and the ship curve of CTS can be drawn above the (1 + k) CF0 curve at the appropriate values of the Reynolds number.

E) The study carried out by the ITTC Performance Committee has shown that the introduction of the form factor philosophy has led to significant improvements in model-ship correlation (ITTC, 1978). For this reason sometimes empirically-derived form factors values are adopted. However, no satisfactory method to derive appropriate values of such form factors has as yet been found.

Questão 189

Observe a figura abaixo, considere as seguintes afirmativas relacionadas à “Wave-Making Resistance of Surface Ships” e responda: (Chapter V - Section 4, p.17-19)




§   



 




I.



At low speeds, the waves made by the ship are very small, and the resistance is wholly due to the waves created by the ship.
 




II.



Since the frictional resistance varies at a power of the speed a little less than the square, when the coefficient of total resistance CT = RT/½ρSV 2 is plotted to a base of Froude number Fn (or of VK/), at first the value of CT decreases with increase of speed.






III.



The general increase in CT is usually accompanied by a number of humps and hollows in the resistance curve. As the speed of the ship increases, the wave pattern must change, for the length of the waves will decrease and the relative positions of their crests and troughs will alter.






IV.



The humps and hollows in the CT-curve are due to these interference effects between the wave systems, and it is obviously good design practice to ensure whenever possible that the ship will be running under service conditions at a favorable speed.






V.



One important point brought out by these experiments (made by Eggert, 1939) is that a large proportion of the wave-making resistance is generated by the upper part of the hull near the still waterline.

This method was developed by Michell (1898) for a slender hull moving over the surface of a nonviscous fluid. From the resultant velocity potential the velocity and pressure distribution over the hull can be obtained.

by integrating the fore-and-aft components of the pressure an expression can be derived for the total wave-making resistance.






III.



to use a combination of mathematical and experimental work and to stress the importance of observing the wave pattern in detail as well as simply to  measure the resistance.

Questão 193

Consider the following statements regarding to “Theoretical Calculation of Wave-Making Resistance”: (Chapter V - Section 4, p.19-20)

§   



 




I.



The alternative method developed by Havelock, in which the wave-making resistance is measured by the energy in the wave system, makes use of the idea of sources and sinks. This is a powerful tool with which to simulate the flow around different body shapes and so to find the wave height, pressure distribution, and resistance.
 




II.



A "thin" ship, for example, can be simulated by a distribution of sources on the centerline plane of the forebody and of sinks in the afterbody, the sum of their total strength being zero. The restriction to a "thin" ship can be removed if the sources and sinks are distributed over the hull surface itself.






III.



Each source and sink when in motion in a fluid, on or near the surface, gives rise to a wave system, and by summing these up the total system for the ship can be obtained. Havelock by this method found the wave pattern near astern, and from considerations of energy obtained the wave-making resistance.
 




IV.



A source may be looked upon as a point in a fluid at which new fluid is being continuously introduced, and a sink is the reverse, a point where fluid is being continuously abstracted.


According to the book “Principles of Naval Arquitecture” (PNA), what is the correct alternative?

A) (I) and (IV) solely are true

B) All of them are true

C) (II) and (IV) solely are true

D) (II), (III) and (IV) solely are true

E) (I) and (III) solely are true

Questão 195

In recent years, a great deal of work on the calculation of wave-making resistance has been carried out in Japan by Professor Inui and his colleagues (Inui, 1980). They used a combination of mathematical and experimental work and stressed the importance of observing the wave pattern in detail as well as simply measuring the resistance. Instead of starting with a given hull geometry, Professor Inui began with na assumed source-sink distribution, with a view to obtaining better agreement between the measured and calculated wave systems, both of which would refer to the same hull shape. The wave pattern and the wavemaking resistance were then calculated from the amplitudes of the elementary waves by using Havelock's concept. Professor Inui tried various distributions of sources and sinks (singularities) by volume over the curved surface, in a horizontal plane and over the vertical middle-line plane. Questão 204

Calculations of wave-making resistance have so far been unable to take into account the effects of viscosity, the role of which has been investigated by Havelock (1923), (1935) and Wigley (1938). One of these effects is to create a boundary layer close to the hull, which separates the latter from the potential-flow pattern with which the theory deals. This layer grows thicker from stem to stern, but outside of it the fluid behaves very much in accordance with the potential flow theory. Consider the following statements regarding to “Effects of Viscosity on Wave-Making Resistance”: (Chapter V - Section 4, p.26)

§   



 




I.



Havelock (1926) stated that the direct influence of viscosity on the wave motion is comparatively small, and the "indirect effect might possibly be allowed for later by some adjustment of the effective form of the ship." He proposed to do this by assuming that the after body was virtually lengthened and the aft end waterlines thereby reduced in slope, so reducing the after-body wavemaking.






II.



Wigley (1962) followed up this suggestion by comparing calculated and measured wave-making resistance for 14 models of mathematical forms, and deriving empirical correction factors. He found that the remaining differences in resistance were usually within 6 percent, and that the virtual lengthening of the hull due to viscosity varied between 4 and 8 percent.
 




III.



The inclusion of a viscosity correction of this nature also explains another feature of calculated wave-making resistance. For a ship model which is unsymmetrical fore and aft, the theoretical wave-making resistance in a nonviscous fluid is the same for both directions of motion, while the measured resistances are different. With the viscosity correction included, the calculated resistance will also be different.






IV.



Professor Inui (1980) in his wave-making resistance work also allows for viscosity by means of two empirical coefficients, one to take care of the virtual lengthening of the form, the other to allow for the effect of viscosity on wave height.


According to the book “Principles of Naval Arquitecture” (PNA), what is the correct alternative?

A) (I), (III) and (IV) solely are true

B) (II), (III) and (IV) solely are true

C) (I), (II) and (III) solely are true

D) All of them are true

E) (I) and (IV) solely are true

Questão 211

In discussing wave-making resistance, it was pointed out that the existence of the boundary layer had the virtual effect of lengthening the form and reducing the slopes of the after waterlines. This is a region where the normal pressure on the hull is higher than the static pressure, owing to the closing in of the streamlines, and the forward components of these excess pressures will exert a forward thrust overcoming some of the ship's resistance. The presence of the boundary layer reduces these forward components, resulting in an increase in resistance as compared with that which would be experienced in a non-viscous fluid, and so is called the viscous pressure drag.

If the curvature near the stern becomes too abrupt, the bilge radius too hard, the after end sections too U-shaped or there are other discontinuities in the hull shape, the water may no longer be able to follow the hull and breaks away, and the intervening space between the hull and the smooth-flowing water is filled with eddies. According to the book “Principles of Naval Architecture” (PNA), a point at which this happens is called a separation point, and the resulting resistance is the third element of form drag, called: (Chapter V - Section 5, p.28)

A) Appendages resistance

B) Wave-making resistance

C) Wave-breaking resistance

D) Skin Friction

E) Separation resistance

Questão 213

To explain the failure of the streamlines to follow the hull it is necessary to consider the variation of pressure and velocity along the length.

The water particles immediately in contact with the hull are assumed to be carried along with it. Due to viscosity, these particles draw the next layer of particles with them, and this effect spreads outwards from the hull. The spread of the boundary layer continues until the velocity of the outer particles at any point is just equal to the potential flow velocity at this point. The boundary layer gets thicker from bow to stern due to the continual entrainment of more water. Within this layer the velocity gradients are very much greater than those existing in the potential flow, and most of the fluid shear responsible for the skin friction must occur within this boundary layer. This leads naturally to the idea of a boundary layer of finite thickness, within which the influence of viscosity is important and beyond which viscosity may be neglected, a concept which has proved useful in analyses of various problems in aerodynamics (Prandtl, 1904). According to the book “Principles of Naval Arquitecture” (PNA), since the velocity in the boundary layer approaches the potential flow velocity asymptotically, its thickness is usually taken to the point where the velocity is: (Chapter V - Section 5, p.28)

A) 0.25 of that of the undisturbed fluid

B) 0.50 of that of the undisturbed fluid

C) 0.99 of that of the undisturbed fluid

D) 0.98 of that of the undisturbed fluid

E) 0.10 of that of the undisturbed fluid

At very low Froude numbers, below approximately 0.10, wave-making hardly occurs, and the free surface at the stern rises to a height approximately equal to V 2/2g, where V is the speed of the ship and g the acceleration due to gravity, in accordance with Bernoulli equation. As the ship increases, however, this rise of the wave at the stern no longer occurs and instead the bow-wave brakes. According to the book “Principles of Naval Arquitecture” (PNA), which answer best fill in the blanks? (Chapter V - Section 5, p.29)




“Especially in the case of bluff hull forms the phenomenon of _____ and _____ have to be considered as well.”

A) eddies / appendages resistance

B) boundary layer / frictional resistance

C) eddies / eddy-breaking resistance

D) wave-breaking / wave-breaking resistance

E) skin friction / frictional resistance

Questão 215

The resistance associated with wave-breaking at the stern has recently been the subject of extensive investigations. Important studies on this topic were made by Taneda, et al (1969), who presented the results of flow observations around a fishery training ship and its corresponding scaled model. Baba (1969) was the first to term the observed flow around the bow "wave-breaking" and presented measurements of momentum loss due to wave-breaking by means of a wake survey far behind a ship model. The most recent work in this area was carried out by Kayo et al (1981). They carried out systematic experiments on the effect of shear on the free surface. They concluded that bow wave-breaking can be considered to be due to: (Chapter V - Section 5, p.29)

Questão 222

For a beam wind, most of the area, both main hull and superstructure, is normal to the wind, and has the same resistance value, so that the effective area is approximately equal to the longitudinal projected area AL. For a head wind, the main hull below the weather deck has a much lower specific resistance than the frontal area of the superstructures. According to the book “Principles of Naval Arquitecture” (PNA), for practical purposes the "equivalent area" can thus be found by adding: (Chapter V - Section 5, p.31)

A) 30% of the projected main hull area to the projected superstructure area, giving the transverse projected area AT.

B) 0,3% of the projected main hull area to the projected superstructure area, giving the transverse projected area AT.

C) 0,3% of the projected main hull area to the projected superstructure area, giving the longitudinal projected area AL.

D) 3% of the projected main hull area to the projected superstructure area, giving the longitudinal projected area AL.

E) 3% of the projected main hull area to the projected superstructure area, giving the transverse projected area AT.

Questão 223

Para um vento de proa, o casco principal abaixo do convés (weather deck) tem uma resistência específica muito menor do que a área frontal das superestruturas.

Questão 224

Hughes developed an equation F = K p (VR)2(AL sin2 Ɵ + AT cos2 Ɵ)/ cos (α - Ɵ) where again F is in newtons, VR is in m/sec, p is the mass density of air = 1.223 kg/m3, and AL and AT are in square meters. For a head wind, Ɵ = α = 0, and the wind resistance will be RAA = F = K p AT (VR)2, with VR in meter per second, so that K = F / p AT (VR)2 which is nondimensional in consistent units. Questão 226

In a second paper, Hughes (1932) investigated the effects of changes in shape, type and arrangement of superstructures, measuring their single and combined resistances by attaching them to the underside of a raft rather than to a specific hull. The resistance of superstructures can be reduced either by a reduction of the projected area, a change in arrangement or by changes in shape and proportions. For ahead winds, Hughes found the principal effects to be as follows: (Chapter V - Section 5, p.32)

§   



 




I.



The total resistance of a number of separate units is in general more than the sum of their separate resistances, because of shielding effects, but these effects decrease with streamlining.
 




II.



Rounding, tapering or stepping back the fore ends of the main erections reduce the ahead resistance, but changes of this kind at the aft ends have little effect.






III.



The shear on the main hull forward has a considerable shielding effect.






IV.



There is probably some scale effect on the resistance of large rounded structures such as masts and smokestacks, but this will offset to some extent (mas isso irá compensar até certo ponto) the omission of small fittings and rigging on the model.






V.



The effects of streamlining will be much less for winds other than from ahead. The relative effect of wind resistance is much greater on the slowest ship.






VI.



The reduction in ahead-wind resistance in calm air by streamlining the erections amounts to about 10 percent.

D) (I), (III) and (VI) solely are true

E) (I), (II), (III) and (IV) solely are true

Questão 232

A resistência ao avanço total de um navio pode ser considerada como composta pela “frictional resistance”; “wave-making resistance”; “eddy-resistance”; “viscous pressure drag”; “separation resistance”; “wave-braking resistance”; e “air and wind resistance”.

De acordo com o contido no livro “Principles of Naval Architecture” (SNAME – 3ª edição: 1988/1989), analise as afirmativas abaixo, identifique quais são verdadeiras e assinale a opção incorreta: (Chapter V)

A) A ship sailing on a smooth sea and in still air experiences a resistance due to the movement of the above-water hull through the air. This resistance depends upon the ship's speed and upon the area and shape of the upper works. When a wind is blowing, the resistance depends also upon the wind speed and its relative direction. (sec.5, p.29-30)

B) The turbulent frictional belt around a ship consists of eddies, so that all forms of frictional resistance are really due to eddy-making. However, the term is usually applied to the resistance due to eddy formation or disturbed streamline flow caused by abrupt changes of form, appendages or other projections, and excludes tangential skin friction. (sec.5, p.27)

C) The wave-making resistance of a ship is the net fore-and-aft force upon the ship due to the fluid pressures acting tangentially on all parts of the hull, just as the frictional resistance is the result of the normal fluid forces. A large proportion of the wave-making resistance is generated by the upper part of the hull near the still waterline. (sec.4, p.15-19)

D) At low speeds, the waves made by the ship are very small, and the resistance is almost wholly viscous in character. (sec.4, p.17)

E) The wave-making resistance and Eddy resistance are commonly taken together under the name residuary resistance. (sec.1, p.2)

Questão 239

The dependence of drag on the value of the Reynolds number has been reasonably documented for most types of two-dimensional foil sections. The figure taken from Hoerner (1965) shows the value of the drag coefficient CD as a function of Reynolds number for various types of airfoil sections of various thicknesses. According to the book “Principles of Naval Arquitecture” (PNA), observe the figure, consider the following statements regarding to “Appendage Resistance” and mark true (T) or false (F): (Chapter V - Section 5, p.34-36)




§   



 




(   )



Below about 1 x 105 the boundary layer is completely laminar. At these Reynolds number values, sections with moderate to high thickness-chord ratios show high drag coefficients because of laminar boundary layer separation.






(   )



Between Reynolds numbers of about 1 X 105 and 1 x 106 the sections show a rapid decrease of their drag coefficient, caused by transition of laminar to turbulent flow which, for moderate to thick sections, separates further along the body (closer to the leading edge), than in the case of laminar flow.
 




(   )



For very thin sections, in absence of boundary layer separation, the transition from laminar to turbulent flow is accompanied by a relative decrease in the value of the drag coefficient. The Reynolds number at which transition of the boundary layer occurs is termed the critical Reynolds number.
 




(   )



After transition of the boundary layer, the transition point remains comparatively fixed for a range of Reynolds number until, at about 1 x 106, the transition point moves steadily ahead, causing the drag coefficient to increase slightly.






(   )



ln excess of Reynolds numbers around 1 X 107 the drag coefficient increases slightly with increasing Reynolds number.
 




(   )



For the sections commonly used in the design of appendages such as control surfaces (NACA series for example), complete turbulent flow is usually obtained at a Reynolds number around 1 x 106 in high-turbulent flow and at about 5 X 106 in low­turbulent flow.

A) (F) (F) (T) (F) (T) (F)

B) (T) (F) (F) (T) (F) (T)

C) (F) (T) (T) (T) (F) (T)

D) (F) (T) (F) (T) (F) (T)

E) (T) (F) (T) (F) (T) (F)

Questão 243

The resistance of rudders can be measured by model tests or calculated from a knowledge of their shape, using drag coefficients for airfoils of similar characteristics and Reynolds numbers appropriate to their length and speed. When rudders are not in the propeller race, the velocity of water past them is somewhat less than the ship speed due to wake effects, but when in the race the latter as a rule more than offsets the wake, and the velocity will usually be more than the ship speed.

Various workers in the field have devised empirical relations for the calculation of the drag of appendages. Some of these relations are independent of Reynolds number and cannot be used for the estimation of scale effects.

According to the book “Principles of Naval Architecture” (PNA), which answer best fill in the blanks? (Chapter V - Section 5, “Control surfaces: rudders, shaft brackets, stabilizer fins, etc”, p.37)


“Peck suggests substituting _____ because of the increased speed over the rudder due to the propeller slipstream.”

A) 1,1 V for V for rudders

B) 2,0 V for V for rudders

C) 1,2 V for V for rudders

D) 1,3 V for V for rudders

E) 1,5 V for V for rudders

Questão 244

Quando os lemes não estão na descarga do propulsor, a velocidade da água que passa por eles é um pouco menor do que a velocidade do navio devido aos efeitos da esteira, mas quando na descarga, a velocidade geralmente será maior que a velocidade do navio.

Vários pesquisadores da área desenvolveram relações empíricas para o cálculo do arrasto de apêndices. Algumas dessas relações são independentes do número de Reynolds e não podem ser usadas para estimar efeitos de escala.
