Questão 252

(PROVA PSPP 2006) O Comandante Raimundo, de um navio petroleiro, tipo “panamax”, com velocidade média acima de 8 nós, em águas parelhas e com coeficiente prismático de 0,86, fez a seguinte pergunta ao Prático Antônio, da Praticagem do Amapá: (Chapter V - Section 5, p.41)

“- Posso lastrar os tanques de ré para derrabar meu navio, considerando que ele anda melhor derrabado?”

Antônio estava com dúvidas para responder ao Comandante sobre a condição de trim ideal que o navio deveria ficar, para fins de obter menor resistência ao avanço, consumir menos combustível e reduzir o tempo da derrota. Antônio fez as seguintes considerações:

§   



 




I)



Geralmente, em navios mercantes, um trim adicional pela proa resulta, quando em baixas velocidades, em um aumento de resistência ao avanço, ocorrendo o oposto em velocidades normais ou de cruzeiro.






II)



Em baixas velocidades, o aumento de calado à ré faz a proa “parecer maior”, diminuindo a resistência viscosa em função da separação da camada limite. Em velocidades altas, ou de cruzeiro, esta resistência é compensada pelo aumento da resistência de ondas irradiadas pelo navio.






III)



Geralmente, navio com coeficiente prismático menor que 0,7, e que alcançam velocidades, cujo o número de Froude seja menor que 0,30, adquirem trim pela popa em baixas velocidades. Esse trim é invertido após o navio adquirir uma velocidade de cruzeiro ou normal.






IV)



A redução de resistência, devido a mudança de trim, que é praticada nos navios de grande deslocamento, é muito pequena e, com isso, a economia de combustível será desprezível para o trecho a ser navegado.


Analise as considerações de Antônio e assinale a opção correta, de acordo com “Princípios de Arquitetura Naval”:

A) Apenas as afirmativas (I) e (II) são verdadeiras.

B) Apenas a afirmativa (II) é verdadeira.

C) Apenas a afirmativa (IV) é verdadeira.

D) Apenas as afirmativas (III) e (IV) são verdadeiras.

E) Apenas as afirmativas (I), (II) e (III) são verdadeiras.

§

Na condição leve, estando o navio em águas parelhas, a razão de superfície molhada por unidade de deslocamento é aumentada e, com isto, a resistência friccional aumenta.

Para um navio VLCC, a razão de resistência total por unidade de deslocamento é diminuída para a condição leve.






III)



O coeficiente de bloco para o navio leve é menor que para o navio carregado e, com isto, a resistência residual é maior.






IV)



A força de empuxo do propulsor é menor quanto maior for o escoamento incidente sobre o mesmo.

For speeds less than V = , the system consists of a double set of waves, transverse and diverging as in deep water, advancing with the pressure point at velocity V.

The characteristic behavior of a high-speed ship in shallow water is illustrated in figure, which shows resistance and trim curves for a model of a destroyer run in different depths of water (Rota, 1900). §

II.



When the critical speed is passed the trim falls off very quickly and the resistance remains almost constant for a time before beginning to increase again but then at a somewhat lower rate than in shallow water.

Questão 271

The increase in resistance that occurs when a ship has a fixed yaw and or a heel angle is most marked in sailing ships and yachts sailing to windward. Steady yaw angles usually occur when the vessel bas to counteract some aerodynamic side force on the superstructure or sails by developing an equal and opposite hydrodynamic side force on the hull and on the keel. For a sailing vessel with completely symmetrical hull the only way hydrodynamic side forces can develop is when the hull adopts an angle of attack relative to its course through the water. ln sailing yachts this angle is usually referred to as leeway. According to the book “Principles of Naval Arquitecture” (PNA), the same aerodynamic force on the superstructure or the sails will also cause a heel angle for yachts sailing to windward. Typical leeway angles are between: (Chapter V - Section 5, p.50)

A) 3 and 6, degrees although on yachts with inefficient keels (i.e. keels which do not possess the characteristic of being able to develop the required side force at small angles of leeway) larger leeway angles may occur.

B) 1 and 3, degrees although on yachts with inefficient keels (i.e. keels which do not possess the characteristic of being able to develop the required side force at small angles of leeway) larger leeway angles may occur.

C) 5 and 7, degrees although on yachts with inefficient keels (i.e. keels which do not possess the characteristic of being able to develop the required side force at small angles of leeway) larger leeway angles may occur.

D) 2 and 5, degrees although on yachts with inefficient keels (i.e. keels which do not possess the characteristic of being able to develop the required side force at small angles of leeway) larger leeway angles may occur.

E) 1 and 2, degrees although on yachts with inefficient keels (i.e. keels which do not possess the characteristic of being able to develop the required side force at small angles of leeway) larger leeway angles may occur.

Questão 272

Heel angles of up to 30 degrees are quite normal. For other ships yaw angles can also occur because of unsymmetrical properties of the hull, such as can occur when shaft brackets or other appendages are misaligned. ln those cases a constant (usually small) rudder angle is required to compensate for the hydrodynamic side force that results. However, in that case a hydrodynamic moment is exerted on the vessel which has to be counterbalanced by the hull adopting some leeway angle. According to the book “Principles of Naval Arquitecture” (PNA), in these cases, however, angles are usually within: (Chapter V - Section 5, p.50-51)

A) 1 or 3 degrees.

B) 2 or 3 degrees.

C) 3 or 5 degrees.

D) 1 or 2 degrees.

E) 3 or 4 degrees.

Questão 273

The early propeller theories followed two independent lines of thought. In the first of these, the momentum theories, the production of thrust was explained entirely by momentum changes taking place in the fluid. In the second, the blade-element theories, the propeller thrust was obtained by analyzing the forces acting on the various sections of the blades and then integrating these over the propeller radius. According to the book “Principles of Naval Arquitecture” (PNA), consider the following statements regarding to “Theory of Propeller Action” and choose the incorrect alternative: (Chapter VI – Sec.2, p.131-132)

A) The circulation theory showed the relation between the momentum changes in the medium and the forces acting on the blade elements, and its subsequent development finally led to the point where it is not only in good agreement with experimental results but also is flexible enough for practical design work.

B) The propeller was idealized as an "actuator disk" or some similar conception, which could cause an instantaneous increase in pressure in the fluid passing through it. They led, however, to the important conelusion that the efficiency of an ideal propeller has an upper limit which varies with the loading.

C) The momentum theories were based on correct fundamental principles, but gave no indication of the propeller form which would produce the calculated thrust.

D) The blade element theories were capable of predicting the effects of various changes in propeller form, but led to the incorrect result that the efficiency of an ideal propeller was unity.

E) The difference between the two groups of theories was not dispelled until the blade element theory developed by Lanchester in aerodynamic research was applied to the propeller problem by Betz and Prandtl.

Questão 282

To develop an expression for the efficiency of an ideal screw propeller with rotation of the race, we assume that the fluid has a translational velocity VA far ahead of the propeller and no rotational velocity, i.e., ω1 = 0. The disk has a rotational velocity of ω, and in passing through it the fluid will acquire some angular velocity in the same direction as the disk. Well behind the screw, the race will have a translational velocity VA. (1 + b), as before, and a rotational velocity ω2.

The total kinetic energy in the race will be increased by the energy of rotation, so that the effect will be to reduce the ideal efficiency. Both velocity components impressed on the fluid are assumed to be uniformly distributed over the disk.

Questão 283

The basic data on lift and drag are generally derived from tests with airfoils of constant cross-section in wind tunnels. These foils are arranged in the test section of the wind tunnel so that they span the section. In this way the measured lift and drag forces at various angles of attack are representative of the so-called two-dimensional case corresponding to a foil with infinite span, for which the distribution of the lift and drag force along the span is uniform. A common airfoil shape used today is the NACA 66 (modified) thickness distribution, superimposed on the NACA, a = 0.8, mean line. When the results of tests on such sections are plotted, a number of interesting points emerge: (Chapter VI – Sec.2, p.136)

§   



 




I.



The lift coefficient CL for small angles of incidence is a linear function of the angle of incidence α.






II.



At some larger value of α the lift coefficient ceases to decrease linearly with α.
 




III.



Zero lift does not occur at zero incidence, but at a small negative angle, called the angle of zero lift, α0.
 




IV.



The drag coefficient remains small and more or less constant for small angles of incidence, but when the lift coefficient begins to fall off, the drag coefficient increases rapidly.






V.



The lift/drag ratio is a maximum at a small angle of incidence, and for such sections to work efficiently the angle of incidence should be large.


According to the book “Principles of Naval Arquitecture” (PNA), what is the correct alternative?

A) (I), (III), (IV) and (V) solely are true

B) (I), (III) and (IV) solely are true

C) All of them are true

D) (I) and (III) solely are true

E) (II) and (V) solely are true

Questão 290

Consider the following statements regarding to “Interaction Between Hull and Propeller”: (Chapter VI – Sec.4, p.145)

§   



 




I.



When the propeller is in its correct location behind the model or ship hull, the conditions are considerably modified. The propeller is now working in water which has been disturbed by the passage of the hull, and in general the water around the stern has acquired a forward motion in the same direction as the ship.






II.



This forward-moving water is called the wake, and one of the results is that the propeller is no longer advancing relatively to the water at the same speed as the ship, V, but at some lower speed VA, called the speed of advance.






III.



The propeller when developing thrust accelerates the water ahead of it, and this has the effect of lowering the pressure around the stern and also increasing the velocity there, both of which effects augment the resistance of the ship above that measured by towing the hull.






IV.



The relations between thrust, torque, and revolutions in open water where the inflow is uniform, cannot be expected to remain the same behind the hull in the variable flow conditions experienced there.

Questão 294

The wake is due to three principal causes: (Chapter VI – Sec.4, p.146)

§   



 




I.



The frictional drag of the hull causes a following current which decreases in velocity and volume towards the stern, and produces there a wake having a considerable forward velocity relative to the surrounding water.






II.



The streamline flow past the hull causes an increased pressure around the stern, where the streamlines are closing in. This means that in this region the relative velocity of the water past the hull will be less than the ship's speed and will appear as a forward or positive wake augmenting that due to friction.






III.



The ship forms a wave pattern on the surface of the water, and the water particles in the crests have a forward velocity due to their orbital motion, while in the troughs the orbital velocity is sternward. This orbital velocity will give rise to a wake component which will be positive or negative according to whether there is a crest or a trough of the wave system in the vicinity of the propeller.






IV.



The total wake is made up of these three components, and in the great majority of cases is negative. Exceptions arise in very high-speed craft.

A) (I) and (IV) solely are true

B) (I), (III) and (IV) solely are true

C) (I), (II) and (III) solely are true

D) (II) and (III) solely are true

E) All of them are true

Questão 296

The wake fraction can be measured in a number of ways. If we are interested in details of the wake pattern, the wake velocity can be measured by pitot tubes, the axial, radial and tangential components being obtained in the neighborhood of the intended propeller position.

As a propeller blade rotates, a section at any given radius passes through regions of very different wake concentrations. We can make the propeller with a pitch which varies from hub to tip in such a way as to suit the average circumferential wake at any particular radius. These average wakes can be found from wake diagrams or can be measured by using vane wheels of different radii which integrate the wake around particular radial annuli. According to the book “Principles of Naval Architecture” (PNA), wakes measured by such methods give the flow existing in the absence of the propeller, and are usually referred to as: (Chapter VI – Sec.4, p.146)

A) nominal wakes.

B) circumferential wake.

C) wake factor.

D) effective wake.

E) effective wake fraction.

Questão 297

This method of deriving the effective wake is based upon thrust identity in open water and behind the model. A similar wake fraction can be derived using identity of torque, and because of the difference in flow conditions behind the model and in open, the values of thrust and torque-derived wakes are usually somewhat different. For model work thrust-identity wakes are to be preferred, because thrust can be measured more accurately than torque. However, for comparison with ship trial results, it is often necessary to use a torque-identity wake, since in most cases thrust-meters are not fitted to ships and the only basis of comparison is on the measured torques. A) one or two

B) two or three

C) four or five

D) three or four

E) eight or ten

B) As a propeller blade rotates, a section at any given radius passes through regions of very different wake concentrations. We can make the propeller with a pitch which varies from hub to tip in such a way as to suit the average circumferential wake at any particular radius.

C) In a twin-screw ship the average wake over the propeller disk will, as a rule, be more than in a single-screw ship of the same fullness, because of the different propeller location, but there will be a considerable concentration immediately behind the ends of the bossings or behind the struts in the case of open shafts.

D) In a single-screw ship for the former the wake is most intense over the upper part of the disk, rather less so down the vertical centerline, and much smaller over the outer lower quadrants.

E) In both cases (single-screw/twin-screw ship), the water flow has a general upward and inward direction, and this gives rise to further asymmetry so far as the propeller is concerned.

Questão 301

According to the book “Principles of Naval Arquitecture” (PNA), apart from the number of blades, the harmonic wake content determines the unsteady forces. This content and the amplitude of Fourier components depend on the hull form and possibly on speed. Hadler, et al (1965) drew the following conclusions on the basis of their results: (Chapter VI – Sec.4, p.149)


§   



 




(   )



The wake pattern is mainly affected by aft body shape. The variations in forebody have negligible effect.






(   )



The radial distributions of mean longitudinal velocity and volumetric mean velocity have characteristics shapes. The single-screw ship with conventional stern provides a near uniform distribution with a magnitude comparable to the model speed.






(   )



The open-type Stern (twin-screw) tends to provide a distribution with values lower at the inner radii and higher at the outer radii. The magnitudes depend on the fineness and shape of the afterbody; in general, the finer the stern, the higher the values.






(   )



In general, for minimizing the cavitiation and Vibration problems, the open-type stern, i.e., transom stern with struts supporting the shaft, is superior to the conventional type for the single-screw ship.






(   )



The rudder may have an effect on the mean longitudinal velocity but does not have an appreciable effect on the derived quantities. The effect of variations in speed appears to be small and the effect of propeller location in the aperture can be important.






(   )



Changes in displacement and trim may result in little variations in wake patterns and, consequently, in the cavitation and vibration charactenstics.

A) (T) (F) (F) (T) (T) (F)

B) (T) (T) (T) (F) (T) (F)

Questão 305

Holden, et al (1980) presented on early design-stage approach to reducing pressure forces on the hull caused by a cavitating propeller. To this end they carried out a statistical analysis of the wake field distribution of 20 slender V-shaped aftbody ships and 49 full-form vessels. Both main particulars and local form characteristics were used as independent parameters. By systematically varying the hull form parameters Holden, were able to indicate requirements for wakes with low peak values: (Chapter VI – Sec.4, p.151)

§   



 




(   )



Large waterline angles and blunt waterline endings should be avoided.






(   )



The stern post half-breadth should be less than 0.05 D for waterlines situated in the range from 0.2 to 0.6 D above the propeller shaft.






(   )



The maximum angle of the waterline with respect to the longitudinal ship axis should be kept below 14 degrees.
 




(   )



The angle with respect to the vertical of the frame situated at 0.1 LPP forward of AP at the waterline through the propeller shaft should be less than 30 degrees. This means that the frames should be Ushaped in this region.

Questão 307

The propeller in open water, with a uniform inflow velocity, at a speed of advance VA , has an open water efficiency given by ɳ0 = T. VA / 2 . π . n. Q0 where Q0 is the torque measured in open water when the propeller is delivering thrust T at n revolutions. Behind the hull, at the same effective speed of advance VA, the thrust T and revolutions n will be associated with some different torque Q and the efficiency behind the hull will be ɳB = T. VA / 2 . π . n. Q

According to the book “Principles of Naval Architecture” (PNA), consider the following statements regarding “Interaction Between Hull and Propeller” and choose the incorrect alternative: (Chapter VI – Sec.4, p.152-153)

A) The value of the relative rotative efficiency does not in general depart materially from unity, being in the region of from 0.95 to 1.0 for most twin-screw ships and between 1.0 and 1.1 for single-screw.

B) The ratio of behind to open efficiencies under these conditions is called the relative rotative efficiency being given by ɳR = ɳB/ ɳ0 = Q0/Q

C) The ratio of the work done on the ship to that done by the screw is called the hull efficiency, ɳH, so that ɳH = PE/PT = RTV/TVA

D) When a hull is towed, there is an area of high pressure over the stern which has a resultant forward component reducing the total resistance. With a selfpropelled hull, however, the pressure over some of this area is reduced by the action of the propeller in accelerating the water flowing into it, the forward component is reduced, the resistance is increased and so also the thrust necessary to propel the model or ship.

E) Quasi-propulsive efficiency equals efficiency behind the hull times relative rotative efficiency times open propeller efficiency.

Questão 324

Schoenherr (1963) dealt in considerable detail with the problem of propeller-blade strength, taking into account the bending moments due to the hydrodynamic loading, centrifugal force, rake and skew. His stated objective was "to derive a formula for the minimum blade thickness required to ensure a blade of adequate strength for normal service conditions." According to the book “Principles of Naval Arquitecture” (PNA), consider the following statements and choose the incorrect alternative: (Chapter VI – Sec.6, p.168)

A) The thickness t was assumed to increase linearly from root to tip, since the analysis had shown that when t is calculated for a section near the hub and such a linear variation assumed, the thickness elsewhere is greater than necessary to maintain Constant stress. It is therefore only necessary to compute t for the most heavily stressed section.

B) He obtained such a formula for the required thickness at any desired section, which can be evaluated when the operating conditions, blade parameters and section coefficients are known. This formula was further simplified to suit the designer's need for a simple working rule.

C) Average values were assumed for the blade shape and section parameters.

D) The simplifications were considered justifiable in view of other unknowns in the problem our ignorance of locked-in stresses and the dynamic effects of blade vibration, ship motion in a seaway, and crash reversals.

E) One representative point was taken on the chosen section at which to calculate the stress this was the point on the face of the blade at the position of maximum thickness, where the stress is a maximum and is tensile for ahead operation.

Questão 332

Consider the following statements regarding to “The Nature of Cavitation”: (Chapter VI – Sec.7, p.171)

§   



 




I.



Cavitation is a phenomenon met with in highly loaded propellers in which, beyond certain critical revolutions, there is a progressive breakdown in the flow and a consequent loss of thrust. In its extreme form, it may prevent the ship from reaching the desired speed.






II.



Before this stage is reached, however, it manifests itself by noise, vibration and erosion of the propeller blades, struts and rudders.






III.



In early days these problems were confined to high-speed ships, but as speeds and powers have increased the erosion aspect of cavitation has become more and more important, particularly in very high-powered, twin-screw ships.






IV.



In single-screw ships, there is a large wake variation over the propeller disk, which encourages cavitation, and it is necessary to give special consideration to propeller clearances from the hull and to the effects of overloading of the propeller in bad weather or when the ship's bottom is fouled.






V.



Avoidance of cavitation and erosion has become an important requirement in the design of nearly all propellers, and it is necessary to consider the problem of cavitation before going on to methods of propeller design.


According to the book “Principles of Naval Arquitecture” (PNA), what is the correct alternative?

A) (II) and (IV) solely are true

B) (I) and (III) solely are true

C) (I), (II), (IV) and (V) solely are true

D) All of them are true

E) (I), (II) and (V) solely are true

Questão 340

Consider the following statements regarding to “Types of Cavitation”: (Chapter VI – Sec.7, p.173-175)

§   



 




I.



Knapp, et al (1970) classify hydrodynamic cavitation in general into the following categories: Travelling; fixed; tip vortex; and hub vortex.






II.



The cavitation forms on marine propellers are mainly of the fixed and vortex type. These types can be subdivided either according to the position on the propeller where the cavitation occurs, or according to the physical nature of the cavitation.






III.



The resulting vortex cavitation is very stable and appears as a thick rope with strands corresponding to the number of blades.






IV.



Classifying propeller cavitation according to the latter method (Vortex type), i.e., according to the physical nature or its appearance, it is possible to specify the following types: Sheet; bubble; cloud; and vibratory.

Questão 344

Consider the following statements regarding to “Detrimental effects of cavitation – Effects on propeller performance”: (Chapter VI – Sec.7, p.178-179)

§   



 




I.



When the amount of cavitation on a propeller blade increases, the flow over the blade is changed. This in turn influences the pressure distribution on the blade, resulting in changes in propeller thrust and torque.






II.



The cavitation increases the under-pressure peak at the leading edge, spreading it out over the chord length. In most cases this change results in a decrease of the lift of the airfoil after an initial increase.
 




III.



The effectiveness, or the lift-drag ratio, of propeller blade sections decreases with increasing cavitation, after an initial increase.






IV.



The described changes in propeller performance, resulting from the presence of cavitation are dependent on propeller geometry and propeller inflow conditions.

Questão 346

Ship vibrations are determined by the response characteristics of the ship structure by the excitation level. Propeller-induced vibratory forces on the afterbody of a ship form the largest part of these excitation forces. It was found that cavitation considerably influences the whole problem of ship-propeller interaction. This aspect of the influence of cavitation has been the subject of significant experimental studies. It was found both to influence propeller blade stresses and to modify the flow ahead of the propeller. The largest effect, however, was found in the pressure fluctuations induced on the ship's afterbody. Not only the amplitude but also the phase angle of the propeller-induced fluctuating pressures are affected. Questão 347

Many criteria have been proposed for predicting the onset of cavitation. The earliest ones, using the average thrust per unit area of projected blade surface, are today not sufficient in many sophisticated designs, although still useful as a first guide. According to the book “Principles of Naval Arquitecture” (PNA), the criterion devised by Barnaby from the Daring trials was to limit the pressure to: (Chapter VI – Sec.7, p.181)

A) 57.4 kN/m2 (8.08 psi) of projected area for a tip immersion of 0.15 m (5.8 in.), increasing this limit by 3.2 kN/m2 (0.44 psi) for every additional 0.305 m (ft) of immersion.

B) 76.7 kN/m2 (10.8 psi) of projected area for a tip immersion of 0.15 m (5.8 in.), increasing this limit by 2.5 kN/m2 (0.35 psi) for every additional 0.505 m (ft) of immersion.

C) 67.5 kN/m2 (9.5 psi) of projected area for a tip immersion of 0.28 m (11 in.), increasing this limit by 2.5 kN/m2 (0.35 psi) for every additional 0.305 m (ft) of immersion.

D) 76.7 kN/m2 (10.8 psi) of projected area for a tip immersion of 0.28 m (11 in.), increasing this limit by 2.5 kN/m2 (0.35 psi) for every additional 0.305 m (ft) of immersion.

§

When the diameter of a propeller is restricted on account of draft or other reasons, the loading factor is smaller, the efficiency suffers and the liability to cavitation is increased. This situation can be relieved by dividing the load between two or more propellers.






II.



Propellers arranged in this way, on the same shaft and trailing in the same direction, are called tandem propellers.

The after propeller, working in the race of the forward one, required a smaller pitch to give the same power absorption.

With both propellers rotating in the same direction, the rotational energy in the race from the forward one is augmented by the working of the after one.

§   



 




I.



They can be located directly behind the ship. Therefore, the additional resistance of shafts and shaft brackets is small, whereas the propeller diameter can be chosen larger than possible below the ship's bottom where often restrictions are posed.






II.



Most propeller designs will produce large cavities in the high-speed range (above 40 knots). When these cavities collapse in the neighborhood of the propeller blade, erosion damage can be unavoidable. Cavities filled with air do not collapse so vehemently.






III.



As the danger for erosion decreases, smaller blade area ratios than for a conventional propeller become possible. This reduces friction, and affects the efficiency positively.






IV.



The skin friction of the propeller blade itself is reduced slightly when compared with a pure water environment.


According to the book “Principles of Naval Arquitecture” (PNA), what is the correct alternative?

A) All of them are true

B) (II), (III) and (IV) solely are true

C) (III) and (IV) solely are true

D) (I), (II) and (III) solely are true

E) (I) and (II) solely are true

Questão 371

Integrated duct propeller is a variation of ducted propeller design wherein the duct is located forward of the propeller and is integrated into the hull (Narita, et al, 1981). A drawback of conventional ducted propellers may be the erosion on the innerside of the duct caused by the heavy implosion pressures of cavitation. A duct forward of the propeller can be of advantage in this respect. The hydrodynamic investigations into this arrangement led further to the following conclusions: (Chapter VI – Sec.10, p.239)

§   



 




I.



Application of the integrated duct propeller results in decreased hull resistance.






II.



The flow into the propeller is homogenized and stabilized.






III.



The duct thrust significantly increases in the behind condition due to inward radial flow around the stern.






IV.



The effective wake fraction and the thrust deduction factor are dependent on the longitudinal location of the duct.






V.



The asymmetrical integrated duct propeller with larger chord length at the top performed worse compared to the symmetrical duct.

D) (I), (IV) and (V) solely are true

E) (I), (II), (III) and (IV) solely are true

Questão 372

The various kinds of motion stability associated with ships are classified by the attributes of their initial state of equilibrium that are retained in the final path of their centers of gravity. For example, in each of the cases, a ship is initially assumed to be traveling at constant speed along a straight path. According to the book “Principles of Naval Arquitecture” (PNA), in this Case, the final path after release from a disturbance retains the straight-line attribute of the initial state of equilibrium, but not its direction. Achieving this kind of motion stability is the designer's usual goal for most ships when steered by hand: (Chapter IX – Sec.3, p.195)

A) Transverse stability

B) Positional Motion stability

C) Directional stability (with complex stability indexes)

D) Straight Line stability (or dynamic stability)

E) Directional stability (with real stability indexes)

Consider the following statements regarding to “Course Stability With Controls Fixed and Controls Working”: (Chapter IX – Sec.3, p.196)

§   



 




I.



A surface ship sailing a calm sea possesses positional motion stability in the horizontal plane (and therefore directional and straight-line stability in this plane) with controls fixed.
 




II.



In the horizontal plane in the open sea with stern propulsion, a self-propelled ship can possess either positional or directional stability with controls fixed because the changes in buoyancy that stabilize in the vertical plane are nonexistent in the horizontal plane.
 




III.



The only kind of motion stability possible with self-propelled ships in the horizontal plane with controls fixed is straight-line stability. This kind of stability is desirable, but not mandatory.






IV.



With each of the kinds of controls-fixed stability, there is associated a numerical index which by its sign designates whether the body is stable or unstable in that particular sense and by its magnitude designates the degree of stability or instability.

Questão 376

The direct or Dieudonne' spiral maneuver is a definitive ship trial (Dieudonne', 1953) which identifies the directional stability characteristics of the vessel. The maneuver consists of the following: (Chapter IX – Sec.4, p.202)

§   



 




I.



The ship is "steadied" on a straight course at a preselected speed and held on this course and speed for about 1 min. Once a steady speed is established, the power plant controls are not manipulated for the duration of the maneuver.






II.



After about 1 min, the rudder is turned to an angle, δR, of about 10 deg and held until the rate of change of yaw angle maintains a constant value for about 1 min.
 




III.



The rudder angle is then decreased by a small amount (about 5 deg) and held fixed again until a new value of ψ is achieved and is constant for several minutes.






IV.



The foregoing procedure is repeated for different rudder angles changed by small increments from, say, large starboard values to large port values and back again to large starboard values.


According to the book “Principles of Naval Arquitecture” (PNA), what is the correct alternative?

A) (I), (II) and (III) solely are true

B) (I) and (II) solely are true

C) (III) and (IV) solely are true

D) All of them are true

E) (I), (III) and (IV) solely are true

Questão 379

The Bech or reverse spiral maneuver (Bech, 1968) is an alternative test to the direct spiral maneuver. According to the book “Principles of Naval Arquitecture” (PNA), observe the figure, consider the following statements and choose the incorrect alternative: (Chapter IX – Sec.4, p.204-205)

A) For unstable vessels, a hysteresis loop is identifiable although a definite relationship is indicated within the loop. This is because the test condition is no longer controls-fixed. The results still provide the shape of the loop for evaluation of the degree of instability.

B) In the reverse spiral test the ship is steered at a constant rate of turn and the mean rudder angle required to produce this yaw rate is measured. This procedure is repeated for a range of yaw rates (from 5 deg per sec port to 5 deg per sec starboard, for instance) until a complete yaw rate versus rudder angle relationship is established.

C) A properly calibrated rate-gyro and an accurate rudder angle indicator are required although in certain cases the test may be performed with the automatic steering devices available on board. If manual steering is used, the instantaneous rate of turn must be visually displayed for the helmsman, either on a recorder or on a rate-of-turn indicator.

D) Using the reverse spiral test technique, points on the curve of yaw rate versus rudder angle may be taken in any order.

E) Results for a directionally stable vessel are similar to results from the direct spiral.

Questão 380

The controls-fixed stability indexes constitute one of the important elements of path keeping at sea. Because the practical problem of path keeping involves repeated instances of path correction, its basic elements tend to merge with those of path changing. Path keeping and path changing ability of a ship depends on: (Chapter IX – Sec.5, p.205)


§   



 




(I)



The magnitude and frequency of any yawing moments and sway forces acting to disturb the ship from the desired path.






(II)



The character of the response of the ship to these disturbances.






(III)



The rapidity with which the error between the ship's path and the desired path can be detected, and with which corrective action can be initiated.






(IV)



The rate at which the corrective action is translated into movement of the rudder.






(V)



The magnitude of the control force and moment applied to the ship by the rudder.

Questão 394

The Nomoto simplified analysis of K and T indexes can be developed from zigzag trial data. These indexes are widely used, simplified analysis tools developed from the linear equations of motion. They are useful in comparing coursekeeping as well as turning abilities. §   



 




(   )



A larger K provides greater steady-state turning ability and a smaller value of T provides a quicker initial response to the helm.






(   )



T has no effect at all on steady turning rate, but a small T would reduce the time required to reach a steady turn. At the same time, the index T is a reciprocal measure of course stability, with stability increasing with decreasing T. However, a negative value indicates an unstable dynamic character.






(   )



The steering quality indexes K and T have an immediate relationship to the conventional measures of ship turning. K' offers a direct quantitative measure of straight-line stability.
 




(   )



with a larger value of K' a smaller rudder angle may be used in achieving a given turning diameter.






(   )



A highly maneuverable ship (with high responsiveness to rudder and both good turning and low course stability) will have a small value of T' and a large value of K'. In other words, a large ratio K'/T', is indicative of good maneuverability.






(   )



K'/T' is a good indicator of course-keeping ability (or good steering). A large ratio K'/T' suggests good overall controllability only if stability is no greater than necessary.

According to Arentzen and Mandel (1960), the ratio of the snap roll in the first phase of a turn to the steady heel in the final phase of a turn may be as large as 3½ for a submarine with a large bridge fairwater and as large as 5 for a submarine without a fairwater. The latter submarine, however, has a much smaller steady heel angle than a submarine with a fairwater. Thus, the fairwater plays a dual role in turning: (Chapter IX – Sec.6, p.213-214)

§   



 




I.



It increases the roll excitation in a turn because of its large influence on the roll moment due to the transverse velocity .






II.



It decreases the magnitude of the roll damping moment,  , and hence, dampens the amplitude of the overshoot of the snap roll.






III.



It decreases the roll excitation in a turn because of its large influence on the roll moment due to the transverse velocity .






IV.



It increases the magnitude of the roll damping moment,  , and hence, dampens the amplitude of the overshoot of the snap roll.

Questão 426

According to the book “Principles of Naval Architecture” (PNA), _____ consists of reducing the ahead power to that level necessary to cause the propeller to rotate without producing any thrust: (Chapter IX – Sec.10, p.261)

A) Coasting with the propeller windmilling

B) Rudder cycling maneuver

C) Crash astern

D) Hard-over turn

E) Zig-zag or Kempf overshoot

Questão 427

Coasting with the propeller windmilling consists of reducing the ahead power to that level necessary to cause the propeller to rotate without producing any thrust. In this case, the ship would be slowed solely by its hull resistance. When coasting with the propeller stopped, the ship would be slowed by its hull resistance plus the resistance of the locked propeller. In practice, the propeller's RPM is likely to be slightly less than its zero-slip value, so that it exerts some sternward thrust. With feedback engine control it may cycle between very slow ahead and astern. A workable measure of coasting behavior is the distance traveled by the ship to reach a specified fraction of the initial steady ahead speed.

According to the book “Principles of Naval Architecture” (PNA), if the propellers are locked instead of allowed to windmill, coasting distances may be reduced by a factor of between: (Chapter IX – Sec.10, p.262)

A) one and three.

B) three and four.

C) two and four.

D) two and three.

E) four and six.

A) Navigation safety often necessitates low operating speeds over the bottom and the resulting low relative speed through water, may be too small to develop adequate rudder force and force on the hull.

B) The effect of current is usually treated by using the relative velocity between the ship and the water, particularly in maneuvering simulation studies.

C) Local surface current velocities in the open ocean are generally modest and close to constant in the horizontal plane. Such currents do not present difficulties for open-sea controllability. Currents do, however, become important in restricted waters where ship operating speeds are often low and currents nonuniform.

Questão 441

Waves can have significant effect on coursekeeping and maneuvering. A ship attempting to maintain a steady course in rough seas experiences wave-induced oscillatory motions in all six degrees of freedom. Roll is also involved in controllability, especially in high-speed ships. Turning can cause roll (or heel) and rolling can affect steering. According to the book “Principles of Naval Arquitecture” (PNA), consider the following statements regarding to “Stability and Control in Waves” and choose the incorrect alternative: (Chapter IX – Sec.12, p.272-274)

A) In the case of head and bow seas, where the frequency of encounter with waves causing yawing and swaying is comparatively low, and where course stability is usually large, serious difficulties seldom occur. The experienced helmsman ignores the high-frequency yawing and steers in relation to the mean ship heading.

B) Yaw-roll rudder coupling often occurs when a ship is turning. This effect is due to the rudder, which produces a heeling moment as well as a yawing moment. Other coupling effects are often experienced when a ship proceeds at high speed in quartering or following seas.

C) In using automatic controls, the tendency is for the system to call for high-frequency rudder movements that produce increased resistance but have little effect on ship heading.

D) Most high-speed ships have a bulbous bow for the purpose of improving resistance characteristics. The increased fore-and-aft asymmetry due to this type of bow introduces greater athwartship asymmetry of underwater hull form when the ship is heeled. Automatic control systems can be designed to help overcome unfavorable coupling.

E) When heel angle is not zero, and ship speed is not zero, then the camberline is not a straight line and therefore introduces hydrodynamic yaw moment and side force. This trend is made more pronounced by the fore-and-aft asymmetry of the hull form, particularly during high-speed operation.

Questão 442

Under seagoing conditions (mar de popa) a number of new factors enter, particularly in the path of long overtaking waves at high speeds, because: (Chapter IX – Sec.12, p.273)

§   



 




I.



Frequencies of wave encounter are high, so that large roll as well as yaw moments can build up.






II.



High-speed ships generally have relatively high static transverse stability (low GM).
 




III.



Significant changes in static stability occur in waves, which affect roll and hence yaw (Oakley, Paulling and Wood, 1974). For example, for following seas with wave crests at the bow and stern, the middle of the ship is in a trough. This portion, therefore, develops much less righting moment; hence large roll angles occur.






IV.



Since the rudder has a large effect on roll as well as on yaw, the design characteristics of the automatic control system are of critical importance (Taggart, 1970).






V.



High-speed ships have relative fore-and-aft symmetry, which changes in rolling.

B) (I) and (IV) solely are true

C) (III) and (IV) solely are true

D) (II) and (III) solely are true

E) All of them are true

Questão 443

Much more serious steering and control difficulties are experienced by ship operators in quartering and following seas. If the seas are approaching from ahead (90 deg < χ < 270 deg), cos χ is negative and the frequency of encounter ωe, is higher than if the seas were approaching from the stern (0 deg < χ < 90 deg and 270 deg < χ < 360 deg). According to the book “Principles of Naval Arquitecture” (PNA), consider the following statements regarding to “Course Keeping in Astern Seas” and choose the incorrect alternative: (Chapter IX – Sec.12, p.274-275)

A) Largely because of the reduction in frequency of encounter, coursekeeping in astern seas usually poses more difficulty than in ahead seas even though it is by no means always easy ir severe ahead seas.

B) In overtaking seas, the waves are overtaking the ship and the frequency of encounter is low. In semistatic case, the frequency of encounter is zero and if the ship is poised on the down slope of a wave at time t = 0 it will remain so poised at time t = t1. In these respects, this is a steady-state, time-invariant condition.

C) In following seas, the frequency of encounter may also be low, but of opposite sign to that in overtaking seas and the ship is overtaking the waves.

D) In ahead seas (that is, seas approaching from ahead) and in situations overtaking seas and following seas, but not in situation semistatic case, the sway exciting forces and the yaw exciting moments acting on a ship are oscillatory with time.

E) It is important to distinguish the three possible situations in astern seas: Vcos χ > Vw (overtaking seas); Vcos χ = Vw (semistatic case); and Vcos χ < Vw (following seas).

Questão 444

In astern seas, the term (Vw – V cos χ) can be very small indeed and hence the frequency of encounter very low.

Largely because of the reduction in frequency of encounter, coursekeeping in astern seas usually poses more difficulty than in ahead seas even though it is by no means always easy in severe ahead seas. For this reason most studies of yaw motion in rough water have been concerned with astern seas.

It is important to distinguish the three possible situations in astern seas: (Chapter IX – Sec.12, p.275)

§   



 




I.



V cos χ < Vw (overtaking seas). The waves are overtaking the ship and the frequency of encounter is low. These are called overtaking seas.






II.



V cos χ = Vw (semistatic case). The frequency of encounter is zero and if the ship is poised on the down slope of a wave at time t = 0 it will remain so poised at time t = t1. In these respects, this is a steady-state, time-invariant condition.






III.



V cos χ > Vw (following seas). The frequency of encounter may also be low [but of opposite sign to that in (I)] and the ship is overtaking the waves. These are called following seas (Mandel, 1960).

A) None of them are true

B) (I) and (II) solely are true

C) (I) and (III) solely are true

D) (II) and (III) solely are true

E) All of them are true

§   



 




I.



Even with controls working, there is a great danger of broaching (turning broadside to the waves) for all ships when their bows are in the crest of an astern wave whose length is 1.5 L or more if the ship is traveling at about the same speed as the wave.






II.



The likelihood of broaching increases as the wave height increases.






III.



A reduction in the danger of broaching can be achieved by a decrease in fin area aft improving the smooth-water controls-fixed stability.
 




IV.



An autopilot with a large control constant, k1, can reduce the regions of instability for the controls-fixed case.






V.



The introduction of a sensitivity to  does not reduce the regions of instability significantly, and an increase in the time lag,  in the controls does not significantly increase the region of instability.

Questão 463

Quando um navio se move através de águas irrestritas em profundidade e largura, as linhas de fluxo não apenas contornam as laterais do navio, mas também ao longo do fundo do navio. Se a água for rasa, o fluxo sob o casco fica restrito, causando maior fluxo nas laterais. Esta mudança no fluxo, por sua vez, altera as forças laterais e os momentos que atuam no navio e, portanto, as derivadas hidrodinâmicas do navio são alteradas.

De acordo com o contido no livro “Principles of Naval Architecture” (SNAME – 3ª edição: 1988/1989), no que diz respeito ao “Effects of Narrow Channels”, analise as afirmativas abaixo, identifique a verdadeira e assinale a opção correta: (Chapter IX – Sec.13, p.282)

A) O aumento na velocidade do fluxo entre o casco e a parede próxima, juntamente com a diminuição da velocidade do fluxo entre o casco e a parede mais distante, cria uma força que atrai o navio em direção à parede próxima e, como mostrado pela experiência em escala real e por experimentos de modelo, ao mesmo tempo estabelece um momento que tende a girar a proa em direção à parede oposta; isto é, a derivada Yy0 é sempre positiva e a derivada Ny0 é negativa de acordo com a convenção de sinais. A magnitude das derivadas Yy0 e Ny0 aumenta à medida que a largura do canal diminui.

B) O aumento na velocidade do fluxo entre o casco e a parede próxima, juntamente com a diminuição da velocidade do fluxo entre o casco e a parede mais distante, cria uma força que atrai o navio em direção à parede próxima e, como mostrado pela experiência em escala real e por experimentos de modelo, ao mesmo tempo estabelece um momento que tende a girar a proa em direção à parede oposta; isto é, a derivada Yy0 é sempre positiva e a derivada Ny0 é negativa de acordo com a convenção de sinais. A magnitude das derivadas Yy0 e Ny0 diminui à medida que a largura do canal diminui.

C) O aumento na velocidade do fluxo entre o casco e a parede próxima, juntamente com a diminuição da velocidade do fluxo entre o casco e a parede mais distante, cria uma força que atrai o navio em direção à parede próxima e, como mostrado pela experiência em escala real e por experimentos de modelo, ao mesmo tempo estabelece um momento que tende a girar a popa em direção à parede oposta; isto é, a derivada Yy0 é sempre negativa e a derivada Ny0 é positiva de acordo com a convenção de sinais. A magnitude das derivadas Yy0 e Ny0 diminui à medida que a largura do canal diminui.

D) a diminuição na velocidade do fluxo entre o casco e a parede próxima, juntamente com o aumento da velocidade do fluxo entre o casco e a parede mais distante, cria uma força que atrai o navio em direção à parede próxima e, como mostrado pela experiência em escala real e por experimentos de modelo, ao mesmo tempo estabelece um momento que tende a girar a proa em direção à parede oposta; isto é, a derivada Yy0 é sempre positiva e a derivada Ny0 é negativa de acordo com a convenção de sinais. A magnitude das derivadas Yy0 e Ny0 aumenta à medida que a largura do canal diminui.

E) A diminuição na velocidade do fluxo entre o casco e a parede próxima, juntamente com o aumento da velocidade do fluxo entre o casco e a parede mais distante, cria uma força que atrai o navio em direção à parede próxima e, como mostrado pela experiência em escala real e por experimentos de modelo, ao mesmo tempo estabelece um momento que tende a girar a popa em direção à parede oposta; isto é, a derivada Yy0 é sempre positiva e a derivada Ny0 é negativa de acordo com a convenção de sinais. A magnitude das derivadas Yy0 e Ny0 diminui à medida que a largura do canal diminui.

Questão 464

When a ship moves through water that is unrestricted in depth and in width, the lines of flow go not only around the sides of the ship but also along the bottom of the ship. If the water is shallow, the flow under the hull is restricted, causing greater flow along the sides. This change in flow in turn changes the side forces and moments acting on the ship and hence the hydrodynamic derivatives of the ship are changed.

If, in addition to being shallow, the channel is also restricted in width, as in a canal, the hydrodynamic derivatives are even more severely altered than they are in shallow water of unrestricted width.

According to the book “Principles of Naval Architecture” (PNA), consider the following statements regarding to “Effects of Narrow Channels” and choose the correct alternative: (Chapter IX – Sec.13, p.282)

A) The decrease in the velocity of flow between the hull and the near wall coupled with the increased velocity of flow between the hull and the far wall creates a force that draws the ship toward the near wall and, as shown both by full-scale experience and by model experiments, at the same time sets up a moment tending to swing the bow toward the far wall; that is, the derivative Yy0 is always negative and the derivative Ny0 is positive according to the sign convention. The magnitude of the derivatives Yy0 and Ny0 increases as the canal width decreases.

B) The decrease in the velocity of flow between the hull and the near wall coupled with the increased velocity of flow between the hull and the far wall creates a force that draws the ship toward the near wall and, as shown both by full-scale experience and by model experiments, at the same time sets up a moment tending to swing the stern toward the far wall; that is, the derivative Yy0 is always positive and the derivative Ny0 is negative according to the sign convention. The magnitude of the derivatives Yy0 and Ny0 decreases as the canal width decreases.

C) The increase in the velocity of flow between the hull and the near wall coupled with the decreased velocity of flow between the hull and the far wall creates a force that draws the ship toward the near wall and, as shown both by full-scale experience and by model experiments, at the same time sets up a moment tending to swing the bow toward the far wall; that is, the derivative Yy0 is always positive and the derivative Ny0 is negative according to the sign convention. The magnitude of the derivatives Yy0 and Ny0 increases as the canal width decreases.

D) The increase in the velocity of flow between the hull and the near wall coupled with the decreased velocity of flow between the hull and the far wall creates a force that draws the ship toward the near wall and, as shown both by full-scale experience and by model experiments, at the same time sets up a moment tending to swing the stern toward the far wall; that is, the derivative Yy0 is always negative and the derivative Ny0 is positive according to the sign convention. The magnitude of the derivatives Yy0 and Ny0 decreases as the canal width decreases.

E) The increase in the velocity of flow between the hull and the near wall coupled with the decreased velocity of flow between the hull and the far wall creates a force that draws the ship toward the near wall and, as shown both by full-scale experience and by model experiments, at the same time sets up a moment tending to swing the bow toward the far wall; that is, the derivative Yy0 is always positive and the derivative Ny0 is negative according to the sign convention. The magnitude of the derivatives Yy0 and Ny0 decreases as the canal width decreases.

Questão 474

Tuck (1966 and 1967) presented results obtained from theoretical studies on the problem of sinkage and trim of ships in shallow and restricted waters. His calculations imply a nearly universal nondimensional curve for sinkage and trim which is almost independent of ship form. Sinkage is defined here as the downward vertical displacement of ship's center of gravity, and positive trim as the bow-up angle of rotation of the ship at its center of gravity.

Squat is the resultant movement due to sinkage and to bow-up rotation. In many cases, trim can be negative (that is, bow down) owing to low-speed operation in shallow water.

As examples of computation of sinkage and trim, Tuck (1967) shows curves of sinkage against Froude number Fn for ratios of canal width to ship length (W/L) of infinity, 3.4, 1.0, and 0.5. Froude number here is based on water depth, Fn = U / √gDW. The ship form used is a Taylor Standard Series with a prismatic coefficient of 0.64. According to the book “Principles of Naval Arquitecture” (PNA), which answer best fill in the blanks? (Chapter IX – Sec.13, p.290)

 

“The value 3.4 for W/L corresponds to the dimensions of the towing tank in which the measurements of Graff (1964) were performed. Clearly, at W/L = 3.4 and for moderate values of Fn, the departure from the result for infinite width is small, but it is already about _____ at Fn = 0.8, even for such a wide channel."

A) 10 percent

B) 50 percent

C) 15 percent

D) 20 percent

E) 25 percent

Questão 477

The purpose in having a control surface on a ship is to control the motion of the ship. Questão 478

The simplest and most common type of control surface is the all-movable surface as diagramed in figure. The dimensions of a typical all-movable control surface are expressed as lying in three mutually orthogonal directions: chord dimensions parallel to the direction of motion, span dimensions normal to the direction of motion, and thickness dimensions normal to both the span and the chord. Since most control surfaces are neither rectangular in shape nor of uniform thickness, each of these dimensions may have several values. Following aeronautical nomenclature, the edge of a control surface adjacent to the hull to which it is attached is referred to as the root and the opposite edge as the tip. According to the book “Principles of Naval Arquitecture” (PNA), observe the figure, consider the following statements regarding to “Hydrodynamics of Control Surfaces: Geometry, Forces, and Moments” and choose the incorrect alternative: (Chapter IX – Sec.14, p.291)

A) The ratio  is the geometric aspect ratio.

B) The profile area, Ar, may be taken as ratio  x  and Δ is the sweepback angle of the quarterchord line.

C) There is a root ­­chord, cr , and a tip chord, ct  and their average is the mean chord,  (for straight-edged surfaces).

D) The ratio  is the taper ratio, ct / cr is the thickness chord ratio.

E) The mean span, b is the average of the spans of the leading and the trailing edges of the rudder.

Questão 479

The total resultant hydrodynamic force in a real fluid is shown in figure as acting at a single point called the center of pressure, CP. In contrast to the resultant force in ideal, two-dimensional flow which would be normal to the direction of motion, the total resultant force in a real fluid is more nearly normal to the centerplane of the rudder. This force may be variously resolved into any number of components.

Three components of interest in ship control are: (Chapter IX – Sec.14, p.292)




§   



 




I.



a lift component, L, normal to the direction of motion.






II.



a drag component, D, parallel to the direction of motion.






III.



a y-component normal to the axis of the ship. This latter component is the reason for having a rudder.


According to the book “Principles of Naval Arquitecture” (PNA), what is the correct alternative?

A) (I) and (III) solely are true

B) All of them are true

C) (II) and (III) solely are true

D) None of them are true

E) (I) and (II) solely are true

Questão 480

§   



 




(   )



Stall is defined as a more or less abrupt discontinuity in the lift versus angle-of-attack curve.






(   )



As the angle of attack on a rudder is increased, the point where the flow separates on the upstream side of the rudder moves forward along the chord of the rudder.






(   )



As the extent of the region of flow separation increases, the slope of the lift curve with respect to the angle of attack begins to decrease.






(   )



if the angle of attack is further increased to a certain critical (stall) angle, there is an abrupt discontinuity in the growth of the lift (or normal force) with angle of attack, and the lift begins to decrease with increased angle of attack.

A) (T) (F) (T) (T)

B) (T) (T) (F) (F)

C) (F) (F) (T) (T)

D) (F) (T) (F) (F)

E) (T) (T) (T) (T)

Questão 482

In addition to scale effect on stall because of different Reynolds numbers, there is the possibility of scale effect because of dissimilar cavitation indexes between model and full scale. Consider the following statements regarding to “Hydrodynamics of Control Surfaces: Geometry, Forces, and Moments – Scale Effects”: (Chapter IX – Sec.14, p.295-296)

§   



 




I.



In contrast to stall scale effect, cavitation occurs at lower rudder angles or speeds on ships than on models run in accordance with Froude's Law.






II.



Froude's Law dictates that the model speed be less than the ship speed, with the model speed equal to ship speed times the square of the scale ratio.






III.



As long as geometric similitude is adhered to, the negative pressure on the downstream side of the rudder in coefficient form, -p/(ρ/2)V2 will be identical between model and ship. This will also be true of the hydrostatic pressure ρgh/(ρ/2)V2 atmospheric pressure and vapor pressure have the same absolute value for both model and ship, hence in coefficient form they will be much larger for the model than for the ship.






IV.



Since atmospheric pressure is usually the larger of these pressures, it delays the onset of cavitation to a higher Froude number on the model than that corresponding to the speed of the full-scale ship.






V.



In any case, the scale effect due to cavitation is not likely to be as severe as the scale effect due to stalling.

C) (I), (III), (IV) and (V) solely are true

D) (I), (III) and (IV) solely are true

E) (III) and (IV) solely are true

Questão 493

De acordo com o livro “Practical ship hydrodynamics” (BERTRAM, Volker. 2ª ed. Oxford: Elsevier, 2012), considere as seguintes afirmativas, identifique as verdadeiras e assinale a opção correta: (Cap.3, p.75)


§   



 




I.



No estudo da interação casco-hélice, a velocidade de avanço do hélice (VA) é, de modo geral, mais lenta do que a velocidade do navio devido à presença da esteira (ship’s wake), a qual pode ser decomposta em três componentes fundamentais: esteira friccional, esteira potencial (Potential Wake) e onda de esteira






II.



Esteira friccional (Friction Wake): A esteira friccional decorre dos efeitos de viscosidade do fluido real, em que a velocidade do escoamento em relação ao casco é acelerada na camada limite, levando à separação do fluxo em regiões de alta curvatura, especialmente na popa do navio.






III.



Esteira potencial (Potential Wake): Sob a ótica de um fluido ideal sem viscosidade e sem superfície livre, o comportamento da esteira potencial na popa assemelha-se ao da proa, caracterizando-se por apresentar velocidades mais baixas com um ponto de estagnação (stagnation point).






IV.



A onda de esteira (wave wake) altera o escoamento localmente como resultado da velocidade orbital sob as ondas geradas pelo sistema estacionário do navio, de modo que uma crista de onda acima do hélice diminui a fração de esteira, enquanto um cavado de onda a aumenta.

A) Apenas as afirmativas (I), (III) e (IV) são verdadeiras.

B) Apenas as afirmativas (II) e (IV) são verdadeiras.

C) Apenas as afirmativas (I), (II) e (III) são verdadeiras.

D) Apenas as afirmativas (I) e (III) são verdadeiras.

E) Todas as afirmativas são verdadeiras.

Questão 494

The main maneuvers used in sea trials follow recommendations of the Manoeuvring Trial Code of ITTC (1975) and the IMO circular MSC 389 (1985). §   



 




(   )



Turning circle test – The trial is performed for both port and starboard sides. The turning circle test is used to evaluate the turning ability of the ship.






(   )



Spiral maneuvers – The spiral test is used to evaluate the turning ability and the yaw stability of the ship.






(   )



Pull-out maneuver – The pull-out maneuver is a simple test to give a quick indication of a ship’s yaw stability, but requires very calm weather.






(   )



Zigzag maneuver - The test was especially developed for towing tank tests, but it is also popular for sea trials. The test yields initial turning time, yaw checking time, and overshoot angle. This maneuver is specifically designed to assess a vessel's compliance with emergency turning standards, using the overshoot angles to measure how fast a ship can initiate a continuous, uninterrupted 360-degree turn.






(   )



The most common maneuver in stopping trials is the crash-stop from full-ahead speed. Crash-stops from full speed are nautically not sensible as turning usually offers better avoidance strategies involving shorter distances. Therefore stopping maneuvers are also recommended at low speed, because then the maneuver is of practical interest for navigation purposes.

Questão 500

As condições de mar adverso incluem tanto as ondas geradas pelo vento local como as de swell. A IMO faz questão de enfatizar que navios atuando na condição classificada como de mar ou tempo adversa podem presenciar algumas combinações de comprimento e altura de onda que, sob certas condições de operação, podem levar a situações perigosas até mesmo para os navios que cumprem o atual código de estabilidade intacta, ou seja, mesmo estando totalmente de acordo com o código, podem existir situações em que o navio pode ficar em perigo. É importante destacar que, apesar da definição explícita de condições de tempo e mar adversas, ela não deve impedir o comandante de um navio de tomar medidas razoáveis em condições menos severas, se necessário.

Ao navegar em condições climáticas adversas, é provável que um navio encontre vários tipos de fenômenos perigosos, que podem levar ao soçobramento ou a grandes movimentos de roll, causando danos à carga, ao equipamento e às pessoas a bordo. A sensibilidade de um navio a fenômenos perigosos depende dos parâmetros presentes da estabilidade intacta, da geometria do casco, do tamanho e velocidade do navio. Isso implica que a vulnerabilidade a respostas perigosas, incluindo soçobramento, e sua probabilidade de ocorrência em um determinado estado do mar podem ser diferentes para cada navio.

A IMO define diretrizes operacionais para que o Comandante evite situações perigosas em condições de mar adverso. De acordo com a circular IMO MSC. 1/Circ.1228 - REVISED GUIDANCE TO THE MASTER FOR AVOIDING DANGEROUS SITUATIONS IN ADVERSE WEATHER AND SEA CONDITIONS, quando o período de encontro das ondas (TE) é aproximadamente igual à metade do período natural de rolamento do navio (TR), ocorrendo variações periódicas significativas da altura metacêntrica (GM) devido à passagem de cristas e cavados pelo casco, manifesta-se o fenômeno conhecido como: (Annex, p.3-4)

A) Banda Estática por Vento Corrente

B) Broaching-to

C) Parametric Rolling

D) Surf-riding

E) Synchronous Rolling

B) Pilot Card, Master's Logbook e Manoeuvring Booklet.

C) Port Information, Wheelhouse Poster e Passage Plan.

D) Pilot Card, Wheelhouse Poster e Contingency Plan.

E) Master/Pilot Exchange Form, Wheelhouse Poster e Manoeuvring Booklet.

Questão 502

De acordo com a Resolução IMO A.601(15) - PROVISION AND DISPLAY OF MANOEUVRING INFORMATION ON BOARD SHIPS, considere as seguintes afirmativas, identifique as verdadeiras e assinale a opção correta: (Page 3-4)


§   



 




I.



A resolução aplica-se obrigatoriamente a todas as embarcações de recreio, todos os navios novos e navios de pesca, independentemente do arqueamento bruto.






II.



O Pilot Card, a ser preenchido pelo capitão, destina-se a fornecer informações ao prático ao embarcar no navio. Essas informações devem descrever a condição atual do navio, com relação à sua carga, equipamento de propulsão e manobra, e outros equipamentos relevantes. O conteúdo do cartão do prático está disponível para uso sem a necessidade de realizar ensaios especiais de manobra.






III.



O Wheelhouse poster deve ser permanentemente exibido na casa de máquinas. A resolução reconhece explicitamente que o comportamento de manobra real do navio pode diferir substancialmente dos dados apresentados devido às condições ambientais, do estado do casco e de condições de carregamento.






IV.



A maioria das informações contidas no Manoeuvring Booklet pode ser estimada, mas algumas devem ser obtidas de ensaios. As informações no manual podem ser complementadas ao longo da vida útil do navio.

A) Apenas as afirmativas (I), (II) e (IV) são verdadeiras

B) Apenas as afirmativas (II), (III) e (IV) são verdadeiras

C) Apenas as afirmativas (III) e (IV) são verdadeiras

D) Todas as afirmativas são verdadeiras

E) Apenas as afirmativas (II) e (III) são verdadeiras
